**Price Sync — cost propagation + deactivation detection, design session, 2026-07-26 (new session):** founder question after reviewing `/middleware/product-manager` — is the price shown there real-time from Gamevion? Confirmed no: `Package.cost_price` is a one-time snapshot taken at promote time (already flagged and deferred on 2026-07-25, see this section's earlier entry and its own pointer at :608), and — a bigger surprise surfaced this session — **zero automation exists anywhere in the codebase**: `routes/console.php`/`bootstrap/app.php` register no `Schedule::` calls at all, so even Stage 1's raw-catalog mirror is 100% manual today. Rather than code straight into this, ran a full grilled design session (`mattpocock-skills`: grilling, codebase-design, domain-modeling) covering scope, trigger mechanism, deactivation semantics, price-propagation direction, audit logging, admin-vs-system reason tracking, UI placement, execution mode, and scheduler cadence — every decision recorded in **[ADR-015](./adr.md)**. Explicitly a plan-only session per the founder's own instruction — no code written this session. Key outcomes: two mechanisms scoped for the next build — **Price Propagation** (auto-applies supplier's price in both directions, no approval, every sync, logged to a new `price_change_logs` table) and **Deactivation Detection** (asymmetric: auto-off when the supplier deactivates or delists an item, but reactivation always goes through a new admin-reviewed **Pending Reactivation** queue — Approve or Dismiss, the latter converting `packages.deactivated_reason` from `supplier_sync` to `admin` so it stops being monitored). New `deactivated_reason`/`deactivated_at` columns on `packages`; new `price_sync_runs`/`price_change_logs` tables; a new async `SyncSupplierPricesJob` (admin-triggered now, scheduler-ready via `PRICE_SYNC_INTERVAL_MINUTES` for later); a new dedicated admin page `/middleware/price-sync` (deliberately not folded into Product Manager). Also surfaced, not this feature's problem to solve: the backend has **no production deployment at all** (still 100% local Laravel Herd, no VPS/cloud, no deploy config in the repo) — real scheduled automation (and reliable Xendit webhooks in production) both genuinely need that migration, tracked as its own separate initiative, not a blocker here.

**Price Sync Center — UI design session, 2026-07-26 (same session, immediately after ADR-015):** founder asked to bring the deferred "Center" UI design forward too, reasoning that a known data model is enough to design against even before it's built. Grilled against two real references — a "Sync Details" per-game modal mockup, and the actual live legacy `admin.keroxshop.com` Price Sync page (screenshot) — used strictly for layout/interaction inspiration, never for its field semantics (several terms needed correcting against this project's own domain model; full reasoning in **[ADR-016](./adr.md)**). Outcome: full 5-section page design (stat cards, Last Sync Details, Pending Reactivation with bulk Approve All/Dismiss All, a new **Manually Dismissed Packages** browsable list, and a paginated Sync History table opening a per-game Sync Details modal), plus one schema addition beyond ADR-015 (`price_sync_runs.triggered_by`). Currency Rate (SYNC-3) reconfirmed omitted — the legacy reference proves it's a real, solvable need for a future non-MYR supplier, but nothing to build against today. **ADR-016 is explicitly downstream of ADR-015 and expected to change if anything in ADR-015 changes during real implementation** — not a frozen contract. Still plan-only — no code written.

~~**➡️ NEXT SESSION STARTS HERE (2026-07-26 pointer, Price Sync design session).** Founder-confirmed next priority: **build ADR-015**...~~ **Done, 2026-07-26 (same day, build session) — see the shipped addendum below and [ADR-015's own shipped addendum](./adr.md).**

**Price Sync — Price Propagation + Deactivation Detection, built, 2026-07-26 (build session, same day as the design session above):** Price Propagation, Deactivation Detection, and the Pending Reactivation queue (SYNC-5/6) are now real code, not just design — 22 new tests added, full suite (350 tests) green. Ships exactly ADR-015's decision #1 scope: `packages.deactivated_reason`/`deactivated_at`, `price_sync_runs`/`price_change_logs` tables, `PackagePriceSyncService` (runs immediately after `ProductSyncService::sync()`'s own Stage 1 — no stage number of its own, since "Stage 2"/"Stage 3" already mean promote-to-catalog and the deferred cheapest-by-name resolution elsewhere in this doc), `SyncSupplierPricesJob` (mirrors `FulfillOrderJob`'s queued-job shape), the `Schedule::call()` entry (`PRICE_SYNC_INTERVAL_MINUTES`, still inert — no cron infra exists yet), and a **minimal** `/middleware/price-sync` page: "Sync All Prices Now" trigger + run-status polling + the Pending Reactivation table (single and bulk Approve/Dismiss). One real bug caught by a feature test before it could reach production: `SupplierProduct.last_synced_at` is a plain `datetime` column that truncates microseconds on write, so comparing it against the in-memory sync timestamp via `Carbon::equalTo()` always returned false — would have deactivated every promoted package on every real sync run. Fixed by comparing whole-second Unix timestamps instead; full story in ADR-015's shipped addendum. **Not built** (deliberately ADR-016's own territory, not ADR-015's): the stat cards, Sync History browsing, Sync Details modal, and Manually Dismissed Packages section — those need their own build pass.

~~Still open, not picked as next priority: the full Price Sync Center UI (ADR-016)...~~ **Done, 2026-07-27 (new session) — see the shipped addendum below and [ADR-016's own shipped addendum](./adr.md).**

**Price Sync Center — full UI shipped, 2026-07-27 (build session):** the full 5-section admin page ADR-016 designed is now real code: stat cards, Last Sync Details, Pending Reactivation (unchanged from ADR-015), **Manually Dismissed Packages** (new, paginated, Restore action), and a paginated **Sync History** table opening a per-game **Sync Details modal**. New backend: `PriceSyncController::index/stats/details`, `DismissedPackageController`, `PendingReactivationFinder` (extracted so the stat card's pending count and the queue itself share one query). One real gap found on contact with the data, not anticipated by either ADR: Deactivation Detection had no per-run audit trail at all — only `price_change_logs` existed, so a past run's Sync Details modal could show price diffs but not which game a deactivation belonged to. Fixed with a new `deactivation_logs` table, same shape as `price_change_logs`; full reasoning in [ADR-016's shipped addendum](./adr.md). Verified live in a real browser (trigger a run, stat cards update, Sync Details modal groups price/cost diffs and deactivations by game correctly, Restore reactivates regardless of live supplier status), not just `phpunit`. Full suite: 361 tests, all green (10 new).

Still open, not picked as next priority: Image Gallery (IMG-1/IMG-2, previous pointer above), ~~`CheckoutController` server-side `player_validations` enforcement (Player-ID Validation dependency-list item 3)~~ (**shipped 2026-07-27, see the Storefront row in §15 and ADR-005's newest addendum**), ~~MUI-7's distinct single-lookup admin tool~~ (**built 2026-08-28, see [ADR-052](./adr.md)**), Gamevion's sandbox `500` (ADR-006 — nothing to do but wait/follow up), VPS/cloud migration (ADR-015 addendum — no code blocker today, but the real reason automation and production webhooks can't fully land yet).

**Founder live-testing pass on the shipped Price Sync Center, 2026-07-27 (same day, real Herd environment, not the build session's own browser check):** founder triggered "Sync All Prices Now" for real against `kedairuncit-backend.test` (Laravel Herd) and reported the button stuck on "Syncing…" with no data ever updating. Root cause confirmed by inspecting the dev `jobs` table directly: **no queue worker was running** — Herd serves HTTP but does not run `php artisan queue:listen`/`queue:work` on its own (that's `composer run dev`'s job, alongside `serve`/`pail`/`npm run dev`; a Herd-served site still needs the worker started separately, e.g. in its own terminal, or via Herd Pro's per-site Queue service). Three stuck jobs (including one ancient orphaned one referencing an already-deleted run, unrelated cruft) were manually drained with `php artisan queue:work --stop-when-empty` to confirm the fix.

Draining that queue surfaced a second, more important real finding: this environment runs `GAMEVION_SANDBOX=true`, and Gamevion's sandbox catalog endpoint returns exactly **one hardcoded simulation product** (`external_ref: 234`, `mlbb5` — the same "Products Simulation (Sandbox)" item ADR-006's addendum already documented for order creation). Nobody had previously connected that limitation to **Price Sync's Deactivation Detection**: because the sandbox catalog never contains any real `supplier_package_ref`, every real promoted Package looks "vanished from the latest sync" to `PackagePriceSyncService`, and gets auto-deactivated — a false positive, not a real supplier-side change. This run did exactly that to 3 real packages (100 Diamonds, 210 Diamonds, 13 + 1 Diamonds), confirmed by their `deactivated_at` all matching the run's `finished_at` to the second. Manually reactivated all 3 immediately (root cause understood and confirmed false, so skipped the Pending Reactivation review step deliberately) — full reasoning recorded as a new addendum on **[ADR-006](./adr.md)**, since the limitation is Gamevion's sandbox environment, not Price Sync's own logic.

**Addendum — refined root cause, 2026-07-28 (client-demo prep session):** the gotcha above ("Herd doesn't run a worker on its own") is real but incomplete. Verified live while building a combined dev-startup script (`scripts/dev.sh`, this session): even when `composer run dev` *is* used — the documented fix — it can still silently drop `queue:listen` if `backend/`'s own `node_modules` was never installed (distinct from `admin/`/`storefront/`'s own installs). `npm run dev`'s `vite` step then fails immediately with `vite: command not found`, and `concurrently`'s `--kill-others` flag (part of the `dev` composer script itself) tears down `queue:listen`/`pail`/`php artisan serve` right along with it. Nothing in the admin/storefront terminals shows any sign of this — it's only visible in the backend's own terminal output, which is exactly why it went unnoticed until this session and can look identical to the 2026-07-27 symptom above (stuck job, no error surfaced anywhere the founder would normally be looking). Confirmed by re-running `composer run dev` fresh and reading its full output rather than assuming the fix from the prior entry was sufficient; fixed on this machine with `npm install` inside `backend/`. Recorded as an addendum rather than editing the entry above, per this doc's own no-silent-rewrite convention.

**Practical rule going forward:** don't trigger "Sync All Prices Now" for real while `GAMEVION_SANDBOX=true` — it will deactivate every real promoted Package, every time, until either a real (non-simulation) supplier catalog is reachable or Price Sync gains sandbox-awareness of its own (not built, not scoped yet).

**Order Resend Delivery (ADR-017) — shipped, 2026-07-27 (build session):** the richer, package-swap counterpart to ORD-7's plain `retryDelivery()`, designed the same day (design-only) and built immediately after. New `order_resend_attempts` table + `OrderResendAttempt` model (insert-audit, same discipline as `price_change_logs`/`deactivation_logs`). New `App\Services\Fulfillment\OrderResendService` enforces same-game-only package swap (decision #1), the order's own `delivery_status=failed` guard (unchanged from `retryDelivery()`), and — only when the game has player validation enabled — a recent `player_validations` row before the resend is allowed (decision #6, reusing `CheckoutController`'s own data-tuple-match check). `platform_profit`/`reseller_profit` are recomputed via the existing `PricingService::calculate()` against the target package's *live* cost values and set on `Order` before `OrderFulfillmentService::fulfill()` runs, so its existing `creditProfit()` (fires exactly once, on success) credits the ledger with the resend's real economics — `cost_price`/`reseller_cost_price`/`selling_price`/`final_amount`/`transaction_fee` are never touched (decision #2), matching the checkout-time flow's own existing "stored profit is an estimate until delivery actually succeeds" behavior, not a new invariant. New `OrderController::resend()` (`POST /api/orders/{order}/resend`) pre-validates synchronously for fast admin feedback, then dispatches `ResendOrderDeliveryJob` (ADR-014 async discipline — never block the admin panel on a live Gamevion call); the job re-derives live package values at actual attempt time. Admin UI: `/admin/orders`' existing detail view gained a single **"Resend Delivery…"** button (`delivery_status=failed` gate) opening `ResendDeliveryModal` — same-game active-package picker **defaulted to the order's own package** (a founder-requested merge, same session: this one flow now covers both "just retry the same package" and "swap to a different package," instead of two separate buttons; the submit button itself relabels "Retry Delivery"/"Resend Delivery" depending on whether the selection changed), a live cost-price reconciliation preview ("Platform absorbs ±RM x.xx"), an inline "Verify Player ID" step that only renders when the game requires it, and an optional note — plus a new **Delivery Logs** table rendering every `order_resend_attempts` row, most recent first. ORD-7's original plain `POST /api/orders/{order}/retry-delivery` endpoint (ADR-014) is unchanged on the backend, just no longer called from this UI. 21 new backend tests (`OrderResendServiceTest` 12, `ResendOrderDeliveryJobTest` 4, `OrderControllerTest` +5); full suite 396, all green (361 at the last checkpoint + 8 Image Gallery + 6 checkout-enforcement tests already shipped earlier this session + this session's own 21). `npx tsc --noEmit`/`next build`/`eslint` all clean. Verified live against a real running `php artisan serve` + real Sanctum token (cross-game package correctly 422s, same-game package correctly 200s and queues the job) — **not** verified live end-to-end through an actual queued Gamevion call, deliberately, since that risks either reproducing ADR-006's sandbox `500` or a real production call depending on `GAMEVION_SANDBOX`, neither of which should happen as a side effect of testing this feature. Decision #9 (cross-game package swap) remains explicitly deferred, not built — see [ADR-017's shipped addendum](./adr.md) for the full reasoning.

**Second-pass resilience & security audit + docs restructure — 2026-07-27 (design-only session):** ran a full read-only re-audit (4 scoped reviews: DB indexing/caching, queue/retry/load-balancing, observability, OWASP-adjacent security) to verify ADR-014's six decisions against the actual code rather than trust the design doc, and to check the security surface ADR-014 never scoped. Result, recorded in **[ADR-019](./adr.md)**: all six of ADR-014's decisions are genuinely shipped as designed; the two things that looked like gaps (no Redis, no Sentry) are the founder's own already-recorded deferrals, not oversights. Real new findings, sequenced by deploy-dependency rather than raw severity — fix-now items (no production-host dependency): missing `orders.created_at` index + a `whereDate()` query that defeats indexing regardless, unverified FK indexes on 7 tables, no idempotency key on Xendit payment creation, no explicit HTTP timeout on `XenditGateway` (unlike `GamevionAdapter`), silent checkout-failure and login-attempt logging, no rate limit on `/api/login`, MFA columns that exist but are never checked, and the Image Gallery upload disk being hardcoded rather than config-driven (corrects this doc's own §15 Image Gallery note — see that row). Deferred-until-production-host items: a real queue-worker process (`docker-compose.yml` still defines MySQL only), Redis for cache/queue, S3 for uploads, centralized log shipping. Deferred-until-real-traffic items: request correlation IDs, queue/error-rate metrics, scheduling the existing smoke tests as synthetic monitoring, client-side error tracking in `admin/`/`storefront`. No code changed this session — design/decision only, per this doc's own established practice for this kind of finding (ADR-013/015/016/017/018). **Also this session:** root `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` (previously generic, unused multi-agent-tooling boilerplate) replaced with project-specific orientation; new `backend/CLAUDE.md` and root `README.md` added — see ADR-019 decision #4.

**Production host chosen — ADR-020, design session, 2026-07-29:** the production-infra gap ADR-015/016/019 kept flagging as "genuinely blocked, no host chosen" is now resolved on paper. Founder had already researched a concrete shape (DigitalOcean pricing calculator, a hand-drawn single-droplet architecture diagram) before this session; grilled one decision at a time (`/mattpocock-skills:grilling`), same discipline as ADR-014 through ADR-019. Governing priority, stated explicitly by the founder and re-applied to every sub-decision: cost is *not* the optimization target — reliability/no-crash, maintainability, and scalability toward an eventual expand-beyond-Malaysia plan matter more, and several decisions below deliberately spend more than the founder's own initial research proposed as a result. Full decision set recorded in **[ADR-020](./adr.md)**:
- **Compute:** one DigitalOcean Basic Droplet (8 GiB/4 vCPU/160 GiB SSD, Singapore region) + DigitalOcean Droplet Backups add-on. One droplet/one region for now — Load Balancer + a second droplet/region deferred until a real traffic/launch-date signal exists, not provisioned speculatively.
- **Database:** moved *off* the droplet to DigitalOcean **Managed MySQL** (reversing the founder's own initial single-box research) — money-critical `orders`/`ledger_entries`/`withdrawals`/`vouchers` data shouldn't share a fault domain with the app processes, and Managed MySQL's optional standby-node failover closes the "droplet crash loses everything since the last backup" gap self-hosted MySQL can't.
- **Cache:** Redis, self-hosted in a Docker container, cache-only — **explicitly supersedes ADR-014 decision #1's Redis deferral** (recorded as a new entry per this doc's no-silent-rewrite convention, not an edit to that one). Queue stays on the `database` driver, now backed by Managed MySQL. Self-hosted (not Managed Redis/Valkey) is deliberately correct here since Redis only ever holds regenerable cache — three explicit revisit triggers recorded in the ADR if that stops being true.
- **Containerization:** Docker Compose in production (does not reopen `ADR-010`, which was scoped to local dev only), chosen for scale/maintainability over minimizing today's RAM footprint.
- **Process supervision:** Docker-native restart policies + Laravel's own `--max-jobs`/`--max-time` flags, no Supervisor — queue workers split into separate services by queue name (`orders` vs `price-sync`) so a slow Price Sync run can never block urgent order fulfillment.
- **Network/security:** DigitalOcean Cloud Firewall (80/443/22 only), SSH key-only + fail2ban. DNS stays at Cloudflare (founder's existing registrar) with Cloudflare Proxy enabled (DDoS protection + edge CDN — directly useful for the international-expansion plan) and a Cloudflare Origin CA certificate (15-year validity, no renewal cron) instead of Let's Encrypt. A DigitalOcean Reserved IP is what Cloudflare's DNS actually points at.
- **CI/CD:** GitHub Actions, fully automated, no manual approval gate — backend fast + concurrency test suites, admin/storefront build/lint/typecheck, build 3 Docker images, push to DigitalOcean Container Registry, SSH deploy, post-deploy health-check with auto-rollback. Confirmed by inspection (not assumed) that this repo has zero CI/CD and zero E2E test infrastructure today. Playwright E2E for the golden paths (checkout, admin resend) is recorded as **decided but explicitly deferred** — the immediate next priority after this ADR, not required to gate the first deploy given the existing 396+ backend tests are already a strong gate.
- **Build sequencing:** Dockerfiles/`docker-compose.yml`/GitHub Actions (everything except the final SSH-deploy step) can be built and proven now, before the droplet is actually purchased — none of it depends on real infra existing yet.

**Explicitly not done this session** (design only, per founder's own instruction — implementation is a new session): nothing is purchased or provisioned — no droplet, no Managed MySQL instance, no Spaces buckets, no Reserved IP, no Cloudflare Origin cert, no Dockerfiles, no GitHub Actions workflow. This ADR is independent of, and doesn't change, ADR-019's own fix-now list (login rate-limiting, Xendit idempotency key, `SupplierCircuitBreaker`, etc.) — those remain separately tracked pure-code items with no infra dependency.

**ADR-019 fix-now list shipped, 2026-07-29 (same day, new session — a read-only project audit turned into an implementation pass at the founder's direction):** every Decision-1 item built and tested except MFA (explicitly descoped, founder's own call — see [ADR-019's newest addendum](./adr.md) for the full reasoning on each item, not duplicated here). Summary:
- `orders.created_at` index + `OrderController`'s `today` filter fixed (`whereDate()` → `whereBetween()`).
- FK-index audit against real MySQL found all 7 flagged tables already properly indexed — but surfaced a genuine, previously-unknown bug instead: two migrations' auto-generated compound-index names exceed MySQL's 64-char limit (`player_region_mappings`, `player_validations`), invisible on sqlite, both now fixed with explicit names. Worth a full migration sweep for the same pattern before the first real production deploy (ADR-020).
- `XenditGateway` HTTP timeout added (10s/5s, config-driven). Idempotency-key research done directly against docs.xendit.co: Xendit has no client idempotency-key header on Payment Request v3; its real dedupe is server-side `reference_id` uniqueness (409 on a genuine duplicate), which already protects same-Order retries. The real remaining gap — a retried `POST /api/checkout` minting a brand-new `order_number` each time — needs a client-supplied checkout-level idempotency key, a `storefront/` contract change not built this session.
- Checkout failures and admin login attempts (success + failure) now logged. `POST /api/login` rate-limited (`throttle:5,1`).
- Image Gallery disk is no longer hardcoded — new `filesystems.gallery_disk` config (`GALLERY_DISK` env, defaults `public`), deliberately **not** reusing the app-wide `FILESYSTEM_DISK` (already set to `local` in this env for unrelated reasons — reusing it would have silently broken gallery's public-URL requirement).
- **`SupplierCircuitBreaker` built** — `App\Services\CircuitBreaker\CircuitBreaker` (cache-backed, closed/open) wrapping `GamevionAdapter` via `CircuitBreakingSupplierAdapter`. Only trips on a genuine server error (`SupplierResponse::isServerError`, new field) — an ordinary 4xx business rejection resets the failure count instead of counting toward tripping, a correctness fix beyond this item's original one-line description. Wired into the real `SupplierAdapter` container binding, so `OrderFulfillmentService`'s actual delivery path is protected, not just a standalone class. `foundation-security.md` §6 checked off.
- Full suite green throughout: 416 fast-suite tests, 4 concurrency tests against real MySQL.

**Live-testing session, Gallery images → storefront/admin `next/image`, 2026-08-12:** founder uploaded a real image via `/admin/gallery` and pasted its URL into `EditGameModal`/`HeroSlideModal`'s `image_url` field (the paste-target workflow §15's Image Gallery row already documents) — surfaced two separate `next/image` config gaps, not one:
1. **`images.remotePatterns` unset** — `storefront/next.config.ts`/`admin/next.config.ts` had no `images` key at all, so `next/image` refused every external URL outright (`Invalid src prop … hostname "kedairuncit-backend.test" is not configured`). Fixed: both files now allow `kedairuncit-backend.test` (https) and `127.0.0.1:8000` (http) under `/storage/**` only, not a blanket wildcard.
2. **`dangerouslyAllowLocalIP` (Next 16 SSRF guard)** — fixing (1) only changed the failure mode, not the outcome: the image request still 400'd server-side with `"url" parameter is not allowed`, logged as `upstream image … resolved to private ip ["127.0.0.1"]`. Next.js's built-in image-optimizer refuses to fetch any upstream host that DNS-resolves to a private/loopback IP, regardless of `remotePatterns` — and Herd's `*.test` domains resolve straight to `127.0.0.1`. Fixed by setting `images.dangerouslyAllowLocalIP: process.env.NODE_ENV !== "production"` in both configs — deliberately dev-only, since a real production backend domain won't resolve to a private IP and shouldn't carry this bypass.

Both are local-dev-environment issues, not app bugs — confirmed the underlying image (`curl`/Node `fetch` with the same `NODE_OPTIONS=--use-system-ca` flag `storefront/package.json`'s `dev` script already sets) loaded fine outside Next's image optimizer the whole time. **Before the first production deploy, `remotePatterns` in both `next.config.ts` files must be updated to the real API domain** — the two entries added this session are dev-only hosts and won't match production's hostname, which would otherwise reproduce gap (1) again post-deploy for an unrelated reason. See §15's Storefront row.

**Same session, also:** `EditGameModal`'s and `HeroSlideModal`'s `image_url` field each got a recommended-dimension hint (800×500px/16:10 for game cards — matches `ProductCard`'s `aspect-16/10`; 1600×800px/2:1 for hero banners — matches `HeroSlider`'s ~1.97:1 desktop display ratio), derived from the actual rendered CSS rather than guessed.

**How to run tests:**
- Fast, everyday suite (sqlite, no Docker needed): `cd backend && php artisan test`
- Concurrency suite (needs the dockerized MySQL): `cd backend && docker compose up -d && php artisan test -c phpunit.concurrency.xml`

**Known gotchas to remember:**
- Tests that spawn a real subprocess to prove locking (the two `tests/Concurrency/*ConcurrencyTest.php` files) must use `DatabaseMigrations`, not `RefreshDatabase` — `RefreshDatabase` wraps a test in one uncommitted transaction that's rolled back at the end, so a spawned subprocess (a separate DB connection) would never see the seeded data.
- Any real end-to-end test of a queued job (Price Sync, order fulfillment) needs a queue worker actually running — `cd backend && composer run dev` (serve + `queue:listen` + pail + vite together), or at minimum `php artisan queue:listen --queue=orders,price-sync,backups --tries=1 --timeout=0` in its own terminal alongside Herd. Without it, dispatched jobs sit in the `jobs` table forever at `queued` — not a frontend bug, not something a browser refresh fixes. **Updated 2026-07-30 (ADR-020 Phase 1 addendum):** since `FulfillOrderJob`/`ResendOrderDeliveryJob`/`SyncSupplierPricesJob` now declare real queue names (`orders`/`price-sync`, no longer the implicit `default`), a bare `queue:listen`/`queue:work` with no `--queue=` flag processes **none** of them — `composer run dev`'s own `queue:listen` call was updated to `--queue=orders,price-sync` for exactly this reason; any other manual worker invocation must do the same or it will silently process nothing. **Further updated 2026-08-26 (ADR-039):** `RunDatabaseBackupJob` added a third real queue name, `backups` — `composer run dev`'s `queue:listen` and `docker-compose.prod.yml`'s worker set both updated to match; the `--queue=` flag shown here is now three names, not two.
- Don't trigger a real Price Sync run while `GAMEVION_SANDBOX=true` (see the founder live-testing pass above) — it will falsely deactivate every real promoted Package.

**Fat-controller audit follow-up, 2026-08-24 (new session) — `VoucherController::issue()` moved to `VoucherService`.** A read-only whole-codebase audit for fat controllers/sync issues/duplicate code (prompted by the founder ahead of the Digiflazz ADR work below) found one real gap: `VoucherController` held a private `issue()` method that wrote the `Voucher` row and debited the platform ledger directly inside a controller-owned `DB::transaction()`, the only money-write path in the codebase not living in a Service class (every sibling — `LedgerService::withdraw()`, `VoucherService::redeem()`/`restore()`/`commit()`, `OrderFulfillmentService::fulfill()` — already does). Fixed: `issue()` moved verbatim into `VoucherService` (now constructor-injects `LedgerService`), `VoucherController::store()`/`storeFromOrder()` call `$this->vouchers->issue(...)` instead; `generateCode()` moved with it. Five tests that constructed `new VoucherService()` directly (`FulfillOrderJobTest`, `ResendOrderDeliveryJobTest`, `OrderResendServiceTest`, `OrderFulfillmentServiceTest`, `CheckoutServiceTest`) updated to pass `new LedgerService()`. New `VoucherServiceTest::test_issue_creates_voucher_and_debits_platform_ledger_atomically` locks in the moved behavior at its new home (no lock/race exists in `issue()` itself — unlike `redeem()`/`withdraw()`, two concurrent calls create two independent rows, so this is a plain atomicity test, not a `tests/Concurrency/*` subprocess test). Full backend suite **637/637 green**, no behavior change.

**Open item found by the same audit, deliberately not fixed this session:** `VoucherController::store()` (Path A, admin-issued standalone voucher) has no double-submission guard — unlike `storeFromOrder()` (Path B), which serializes on `vouchers.order_id`'s unique index, a standalone voucher has no natural equivalence key (`order_id` is null), so two rapid identical submissions genuinely create two separate valid-looking `Voucher` rows, each with its own ledger debit. Low severity (admin-only action, audit trail visible, no customer-facing exploit), but a real fix requires picking a design first — an idempotency key from the admin frontend + unique constraint, accepting the risk as-is, or a short debounce lock — not just "add a test" the way Path B's gap would be. Founder's call: park it here, grill the tradeoff via `/mattpocock-skills:grilling` in a future session before building any of the three options.

**ADR-031 (multi-supplier routing) built, 2026-08-25 (new session), test-first — first of the Digiflazz build sequence (ADR-031 → 032 → 030 → 033 → 034, review found the ADRs solid before starting).** New `App\Services\Supplier\SupplierAdapterFactory` (mirrors `PaymentGatewayFactory`) + `UnsupportedSupplierException`. `AppServiceProvider`'s single global `SupplierAdapter::class` binding (non-e2e) removed, replaced with `supplier-adapter.gamevion` — the e2e branch keeps its own `SupplierAdapter::class` binding unchanged (decision #3) plus a new `supplier-adapter.e2e-fake-supplier` binding matching `E2ESeeder`'s real slug. `OrderFulfillmentService::fulfill()` now resolves its adapter via `$order->supplier->slug` through the factory (constructor takes `SupplierAdapterFactory`, not a fixed `SupplierAdapter`) — new guard throws `OrderFulfillmentException` if `supplier_id` is null. `SyncSupplierProductsCommand`/`SyncSupplierPricesJob` loop every active `Supplier` row, each resolved via the factory; one supplier's failure (unbound adapter or its own sync exception) is logged and skipped, not fatal to the run — `SyncSupplierPricesJob`'s stats now sum across every supplier that completed, and the run is only marked `failed` if all active suppliers failed (a build-time judgment call, not specified in the ADR). `OrderResendService` needed no change — it already delegates to `fulfill()` after updating `order.supplier_id` to the target package's own supplier. Two production call sites that construct `OrderFulfillmentService` directly (`SandboxOrderController::resend()`, the `app:order-fulfillment-test-fulfill` concurrency-test command) now bind their fake adapter under the real target supplier's slug before resolving the factory, instead of relying on a single global binding. ~9 test files' order/fixture helpers updated to carry a real `supplier_id` (the concurrency suite's `OrderFulfillmentConcurrencyTest` included — re-verified green against real MySQL, not just the fast sqlite suite). Full backend suite **645/645 green** (up from 637), concurrency suite **5/5 green**. No admin/storefront changes — backend-only.

**ADR-032 (async supplier delivery — `DeliveryStatus::Pending` + normalized `SupplierResponse` outcome + webhook & poll finalization) built, 2026-08-25 (same session), test-first — second of the Digiflazz build sequence.** Built the generic, supplier-agnostic mechanism only — the actual `DigiflazzWebhookController` was deliberately deferred, originally reasoned as "ADR-030's own build pass" (it needs a real `DigiflazzAdapter`/webhook secret to wire against, which didn't exist yet at this point in the session). **Correction, later the same session: ADR-030's own build pass did not include it either** — `DigiflazzAdapter` shipped but the webhook controller/route still doesn't exist anywhere in the codebase. Genuinely still open, no longer tied to a specific future ADR pass — see the standing pointer below.

New `DeliveryStatus::Pending` case (`Processing→Pending`, `Pending→Delivered`, `Pending→Failed` via new `OrderStatusService::markPending()`/`finalizePendingSuccess()`/`finalizePendingFailure()`); `markNeedsReview()` additionally accepts `Pending` as a source state (decision 6 — a Pending order too old to safely re-poll, per Digiflazz's 90-day re-submit rule, is flagged for manual review the same way ADR-026's ambiguous-failure cases are). New `App\Services\Supplier\SupplierOutcome` enum (`Success`/`Pending`/`Failure`) — `SupplierResponse` gained a `pending()` factory + an additive `outcome` property (defaults to `Failure` for full backward compatibility; `->success` stays `false` for `pending()`, matching every pre-ADR-032 caller that only ever checked it). `OrderFulfillmentService::fulfill()` now branches on `$result->outcome`, never `->success` alone — a `Pending` response parks the order (no ledger credit, no voucher commit) rather than being mistaken for a failure. New `OrderFulfillmentService::finalizePendingDelivery(Order, SupplierOutcome, ?supplierRef, ?supplierResponse)` — the one money path out of Pending, same lock-then-guard idempotency discipline as `fulfill()` itself (a second call on an already-finalized order throws, doesn't silently no-op); a `Failure` outcome lands on the plain `Failed` state so the existing Failed-only voucher gate (`VoucherController::storeFromOrder()`) applies unchanged, no new double-compensation guard needed. New `App\Jobs\CheckSupplierDeliveryJob` (poll backup, decision 5) — resolves the order's adapter via the ADR-031 factory, calls `checkStatus($order->reference_number)` (not `supplier_ref`, which a genuinely-Pending order may not have yet — Digiflazz's own `checkStatus` re-submits keyed on our own `ref_id`), routes the outcome through `finalizePendingDelivery()`, swallows an already-advanced order the same way `FulfillOrderJob` does. `ReconcilePendingDeliveriesCommand` gained a third branch (`checkStalePending()`) dispatching that job for stale `Pending` orders, with per-supplier `pending_stale_minutes`/`max_reconcile_age_days` thresholds read from `Supplier.api_config` and falling back to new `config('services.delivery_reconciliation.*')` defaults (10 min / 90 days) — never calls the supplier synchronously from the scheduled command (ADR-014).

New concurrency test (`FinalizePendingDeliveryConcurrencyTest`, real MySQL, mirrors `OrderFulfillmentConcurrencyTest`'s own subprocess-race pattern via a new `app:order-finalize-pending-delivery-test-finalize` test-only command) proves the idempotency guard for real — exactly one of two simultaneous finalize attempts succeeds, exactly two `order_profit` ledger entries written, not four.

Frontend exhaustiveness pass (ADR-032's own consequence checklist, same discipline ADR-026 established): `admin/src/lib/orders.ts`'s `delivery_status`/`OrderStatusFilter` types gained `"pending"`/`"pending_delivery"`; `/admin/orders` gained a "Pending (Supplier)" filter (backend: `OrderController::index()`'s `pending_delivery` case) and a distinct `info`-colored badge (deliberately different from `processing`'s `warning`, so an admin can tell at a glance this is waiting on an async supplier); `middleware/sandbox/page.tsx`'s own copy of the color map updated too (type-only — sandbox's `FakeSupplierAdapter` can't structurally produce a `Pending` outcome). Storefront: `StatusBadge`/`OrderStatusTracker` render `Pending` with the exact same customer-facing copy as `Processing` ("topup sedang diproses" — decision's own wording), never a distinct customer-visible state.

30 new/changed backend tests across `OrderStatusServiceTest` (+9), new `SupplierResponseTest` (3), `OrderFulfillmentServiceTest` (+7), new `CheckSupplierDeliveryJobTest` (5), `ReconcilePendingDeliveriesCommandTest` (+4), `OrderControllerTest` (+1), plus the new concurrency test; full backend suite **671/671 green** (up from 645), concurrency suite **6/6 green** (up from 5). `npx tsc --noEmit`/`next build` clean on both `admin/` and `storefront/`; the pre-existing `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` findings on `admin/orders/page.tsx`/`middleware/sandbox/page.tsx` confirmed unrelated (same lines already flagged before this session, per the 2026-08-14 precedent for the same files).

**ADR-030 (Digiflazz buyer-role adapter) built, 2026-08-25 (same session), test-first — third of the Digiflazz build sequence.** Every request/response shape verified against the real live docs (developer.digiflazz.com) while building, not assumed from the ADR's own text alone — one real gap found and fixed as a prerequisite, described below.

**Prerequisite fix found while building, not anticipated by ADR-030/032's own text: `SupplierAdapter::checkStatus()` widened from a bare `string $supplierRef` to a new `SupplierStatusCheckRequest` value object.** Confirmed live in Digiflazz's real docs: their check-status is a literal re-submit of the original topup request ("Cek status dapat dilakukan dengan melakukan topup ulang dengan ref id yang sama") — it requires the original `buyer_sku_code` **and** `customer_no`, not the ref_id alone, which a single string parameter can't carry. Additive for Gamevion (only `supplierRef` is ever read there; `productRef`/`playerId`/`serverId` stay null and unused) — its own behavior is unchanged. Blast radius was every class implementing `SupplierAdapter`, including test doubles (~14 files) — all mechanical signature updates, no logic changes; `CheckSupplierDeliveryJob` now builds the full request from the order's own `reference_number`/`supplier_product_ref`/`player_id`/`server_id`.

New `App\Services\Supplier\Digiflazz\DigiflazzAdapter` — `checkBalance()`→`/v1/cek-saldo`, `listProducts()`→`/v1/price-list` (`cmd:prepaid`), `createOrder()`/`checkStatus()`→`/v1/transaction` (share one private `submitTransaction()`, since Digiflazz's own docs describe checkStatus as literally re-submitting the same request shape), `validatePlayer()`→`ValidationNotSupportedException` (ADR-005, no such endpoint exists). Per-endpoint MD5 signature confirmed for real: `md5(username+apiKey+"depo")` for cek-saldo (live-verified via research this session, matching the ADR's own 2026-08-24 re-check correction), separate literal-word suffixes for price-list/transaction. Three-way transaction outcome (`Sukses`/`Pending`/`Gagal`, rc `00`/`03`/`02`) maps to ADR-032's `SupplierOutcome` — confirmed HTTP is always 200 for a well-formed request, with `rc` inside `data` carrying the real signal even for an auth-level rejection (matches ADR-030's own live-confirmed `rc: 45` IP-whitelist finding). `listProducts()` normalizes Digiflazz's real boolean `buyer_product_status` into the same `'active'`/`'inactive'` string convention `GamevionAdapter` already uses, since `PendingReactivationFinder` reads `SupplierProduct.status_raw` as a string uniformly across suppliers. `normalizeCustomerNo()` is config-driven (`DIGIFLAZZ_CUSTOMER_NO_SEPARATOR`, default `|`) — the real per-game format isn't publicly documented, corrected at `app:digiflazz-smoke-test` time once the account is live, per the ADR's own decision 5.

`ProductSyncService` decision 2 (business filter lives in sync, not the adapter) built: new private `applyCategoryWhitelist()` reads `Supplier.api_config['category_whitelist']`, filters before writing `supplier_products` rows — empty/absent whitelist means no filtering (Gamevion's own existing behavior, unchanged, proven by a dedicated regression test). `AppServiceProvider` gained `supplier-adapter.digiflazz` (same `CircuitBreakingSupplierAdapter` wrapping shape as `gamevion`, independent breaker named `'digiflazz'`). New `app:digiflazz-smoke-test` (mirrors `GamevionSmokeTest`, but — unlike it — deliberately DOES call `createOrder()`, since ADR-030 decision 7's four official test cases are designed specifically to be called for real, side-effect-free, the same way Stripe's test card numbers are) — **not yet run live**: the Digiflazz account is still IP-whitelist-blocked (ADR-030's own Context, `rc: 45`), so `normalizeCustomerNo()`'s separator and every response shape here stay `Http::fake()`-verified-only until that clears, same "not yet verified live" caveat CHIP currently carries.

24 new/changed backend tests: new `DigiflazzAdapterTest` (18), `ProductSyncServiceTest` (+2), `SupplierAdapterFactoryTest` (+1), plus the mechanical `checkStatus()` signature migration across `GamevionAdapterTest`'s 2 direct calls (behavior-unchanged, no new assertions). Full backend suite **692/692 green** (up from 671), concurrency suite unaffected, still **6/6 green**. No admin/storefront changes — backend-only, and no `Supplier` DB row for `'digiflazz'` created yet (deliberately — that's the founder's own real-account provisioning step, tracked separately from this code).

**ADR-033 (foreign-currency supplier pricing, FX-rate conversion at sync time) + its FX Rate History addendum built, 2026-08-25 (same session), test-first — fourth of the Digiflazz build sequence, grilled first (the addendum was the founder's own request: "kita patut ada history juga... sebab dari situ kita boleh tahu setiap perubahan rate tu ikut rate mana").** Real API response shape verified live before coding (`open.er-api.com/v6/latest/IDR` fetched directly this session, not assumed) — `result`/`base_code`/`rates.{CODE}` fields confirmed.

New `currency_rates` table (append-only, keyed by `(from, to, fetched_at)`, matches `price_change_logs`/`deactivation_logs`'s own convention — no FK to `suppliers`, a currency pair isn't one supplier's property) + `App\Models\CurrencyRate`. New `App\Services\Currency\CurrencyRateService::rate($from, $to)` — reads `config('services.fx_api')` directly (no per-instance constructor params, unlike `SupplierAdapter`/`PaymentGateway`, since this is one app-wide service not a per-supplier implementation); `Cache::remember()`s the live fetch (`services.fx_api.cache_ttl_seconds`, default 24h) and falls back to the last-known stored rate when the live call fails or returns a response missing the requested `to` currency; throws a new `CurrencyRateUnavailableException` only when no rate was ever stored for that pair at all (the one case with nothing to fall back to). `rate($from, $to)` returns "how many `$to` per 1 `$from`" (e.g. `rate('IDR','MYR')` = 0.000228) — callers multiply, a deliberate build-time clarification of the ADR's own pseudocode (`price / rate`), which assumed the inverse direction; both are equivalent, this just matches the method's own signature more literally and avoids near-zero-divisor confusion.

`ProductSyncService` decision 3 (single conversion boundary) built: constructor now takes `CurrencyRateService` (auto-wired everywhere except its own test, which resolves one directly — zero call-site changes needed at `SyncSupplierProductsCommand`/`SyncSupplierPricesJob`, both already container-resolved). `MYR` stays `round()`, unchanged; a non-MYR supplier's price is `ceil(price * rate * 100)` — `ceil`, never `round`, protects margin by construction per the founder's own decision. A `CurrencyRateUnavailableException` (no rate ever stored, live fetch also failing) surfaces as the same `ProductSyncFailedException` an adapter failure already produces — this run writes nothing for that supplier rather than silently mis-pricing every item, and (per ADR-031's own per-supplier isolation) never blocks any other supplier's sync in the same run. New `ProductSyncResult::$fxRateUsed` (`{from,to,rate,source}|null`) threads the rate actually used back to the caller.

Addendum decision 3 (array from day one) built in `SyncSupplierPricesJob`: `PriceSyncRun.stats` gained `fx_rates_used: []`, one entry appended per non-MYR supplier that completed in that run — a MYR-only run (Gamevion alone, today's only real case) simply leaves it empty. Addendum decisions 1/2/4 (two surfaces, generic, paginated) built: new `Middleware\CurrencyRateController::index()` (read-only, no write action — there's no admin-editable rate) at `GET /api/middleware/price-sync/fx-rates`, same `super_admin`-gated tier as the rest of Price Sync Center, paginated newest-first. Admin: new `FxRateHistorySection.tsx` (own component, same extraction precedent as `PendingPriceChangeSection`/`SyncDetailsModal`) rendered below Sync History; a new "Rate used: 1 IDR = 0.000228 MYR" line in Last Sync Details, shown only when `stats.fx_rates_used` is non-empty. `/middleware/price-sync`'s own top comment (previously: "Currency Rate (SYNC-3) is deliberately omitted per ADR-016 decision #6") updated to point at this shipped addendum instead of silently going stale.

23 new/changed backend tests: new `CurrencyRateServiceTest` (6), `ProductSyncServiceTest` (+4: conversion, MYR-null, FX-unavailable-failure, plus the two existing whitelist tests updated for a real faked FX call), `SyncSupplierPricesJobTest` (+2, plus 1 existing test's fixture price corrected to a realistic IDR magnitude — 11.0 "IDR" was producing a sub-1-sen converted price that tripped ADR-025's own swing guard, a test-data bug from before real conversion existed, not a logic bug), new `CurrencyRateControllerTest` (4). Full backend suite **707/707 green** (up from 692), concurrency suite unaffected, still **6/6 green**. `npx tsc --noEmit`/`eslint`/`next build` clean on `admin/`; the one `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` finding on `/middleware/price-sync/page.tsx` confirmed pre-existing and untouched by this session's diff. No `currency_rates` rows exist in any real environment yet — same as `DigiflazzAdapter` itself, this fills in once a real Digiflazz sync actually runs.

**ADR-034 (storefront best-price selection, `packages.denomination`) built, 2026-08-25 (new session), test-first — last item in the 031→032→030→033→034 Digiflazz sequence.** No open questions were recorded against this ADR, so no re-check pass was needed before coding.

New nullable `packages.denomination` (unsigned int, `Package` fillable+cast) — the equivalence key for dedup is `(game_id, denomination)`, never denomination alone. Two curation points, matching decision 3's "promote or edit time" wording: `PromoteSupplierProductRequest`/`SupplierProductController::promote()` gained an optional `denomination` field (null when omitted, promotes exactly as before); a new dedicated `PATCH /api/packages/{package}/denomination` (`UpdatePackageDenominationRequest` + `PackageController::updateDenomination()`) mirrors the existing `updateMarkup`/`updateStatus` per-field-endpoint pattern rather than folding into the rename-only `UpdatePackageRequest` — this is how an admin backfills the key onto an already-promoted package (e.g. the existing Gamevion row once a matching Digiflazz one exists).

`CatalogController::packages()` gained a private `dedupByDenomination()`: active packages are partitioned into `denomination !== null` (grouped by `denomination` — `game_id` is already fixed, since this method only ever runs against one game's own package query — cheapest selling price kept, tie-broken by lower package id for determinism) and `denomination === null` (never grouped — passed through untouched, so non-integer-amount products like bundles/passes are unaffected). `CatalogController::index()`'s own cheapest-price-per-game calc needed no change — it was already a `min()` across all active packages regardless of duplicates.

Admin UI: `/admin/games`' package table gained a **Denomination** column with an inline `DenominationCell` (same fresh-mount-per-value-change pattern as the existing `MarkupCell`, empty input clears back to null) — matches this screen's own documented "inline per-row controls, not a modal-per-field" convention, so denomination was deliberately kept out of `EditPackageModal` (rename+delete only). `/middleware/product-manager`'s `PromoteProductModal` gained an optional Denomination field at promote time.

10 new/changed backend tests: `PackageControllerTest` (+5: set/clear/reject-zero/reject-negative/requires-auth on the new endpoint), `SupplierProductControllerTest` (+2: promote with/without denomination), `CatalogControllerTest` (+3: dedup keeps cheaper, null packages never deduped, deterministic tie-break). Full backend suite **717/717 green** (up from 707), migration run against the local dev DB (per this repo's own migration gotcha). `npx tsc --noEmit`/`eslint`/`next build` clean on `admin/` — the pre-existing `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` findings are all in files this session didn't touch (confirmed by diffing the lint output against changed files). No storefront changes needed — the dedup happens server-side before the response ever reaches it.

**Follow-up, same session, founder feedback while reviewing the live `/admin/games` and `/middleware/product-manager` screens: package listings now sort by denomination ascending, not alphabetically by name.** The founder's own reasoning — smaller denomination usually means cheaper, so it's the more useful default order than string-sorting names (which put "10209 Diamonds" before "1192 Diamonds"). Applied at both listing endpoints admin uses (`GameController::packages()`, which already powers both `/admin/games`' package table and `/middleware/product-manager`'s Catalog tab — one change point) and the public `CatalogController::packages()` (storefront) — all three screens confirmed to read from just these two endpoints, no client-side sort anywhere to also touch. Packages without a curated `denomination` yet sort last (by name), never first, since an unset value has no known "size" to sort by. `CatalogController::packages()`'s dedup final sort also switched from `sort_order`/name to the same denomination-ascending/name-fallback ordering — `sort_order` is no longer read by this endpoint at all (still used elsewhere, e.g. hero slides — untouched). 2 new tests (`GameControllerTest`, `CatalogControllerTest`, one each, both asserting the exact same 4-package ordering including a null-denomination package sorting last). Full backend suite **719/719 green**.

**Reports built (RPT-1..3), 2026-08-26 — grilled before coding, this session.** Founder asked for an easy backlog admin feature depending only on already-solid logic; Reports (read-only, aggregates existing `Order`/ledger data, no new business logic) was picked over Customer Analytics (needs new segmentation rules) and Reviews (no `Review` model exists yet). A separate ask — an LLM natural-language query assistant on top of Reports — was deliberately scoped out to a future ADR+grilling pass (see `project_llm_report_assistant_backlog` memory); not built this session.

Grilling surfaced a real gap before any code was written: `Order.platform_profit`/`reseller_profit` are stamped at checkout time, before the delivery outcome is known, but the ledger only credits `order_profit` on a *successful delivery* (`OrderFulfillmentService`) — an order that gets paid, fails delivery, and becomes a store-credit Voucher instead (ADR-004) would silently overstate profit if the cached column were trusted. Every RPT figure was pinned before coding: **Sales**/**Orders Count**/**Latest Order** = `payment_status=Paid` orders scoped by `paid_at` (never `created_at`); **Owner Profit** = summed from `ledger_entries` (`type=order_profit`), mirroring WTH-4's own "ledger, not a cached balance" precedent; **Margin %** = platform profit / sales; daily trend (RPT-2) buckets by the order's own `paid_at` day in **Asia/Kuala_Lumpur**, both series overlaid so a day's sales and its (eventually-recognized) profit read together. Reports is reseller-aware from the start (`reseller_id` filter, default combined view, platform/reseller profit shown as two separate numbers) — a deliberate "prefer solid over scalable" call since the founder is actively onboarding a real reseller partnership.

New `orders.paid_at` (nullable timestamp, +composite index) set at the exact instant `payment_status` becomes Paid — `XenditWebhookController`, `ChipWebhookController`, `ReconcilePendingPaymentsCommand`, `CheckoutService`'s voucher-fully-covered path, and `SandboxOrderController`. New `ReportService` (all query logic, no calculation in the controller) + `Admin\ReportController` (`GET /api/reports/{resellers,summary,trend,export}`, same `admin.role:super_admin,admin` tier as Orders/Withdrawals — both already expose profit fields). RPT-3 export added `barryvdh/laravel-dompdf` (new dependency) for CSV/PDF, one row per order for the selected scope.

Admin UI: new `/admin/reports` (PrimeReact-Tailwind only, ADR-038 — new `components/ui/select.tsx` PrimeReact `Select` wrapper, first use of it in the codebase) — RPT-1 stat cards, RPT-2 daily trend (hand-rolled inline SVG, no charting library added — built per the `dataviz` skill's procedure: multi-line form, single shared y-axis, categorical palette validated with the skill's own CVD/contrast checker, legend + end-labels + a "View as table" fallback as the required relief for the light-mode aqua-vs-surface contrast WARN), RPT-3 year/month filter + reseller filter + CSV/PDF export buttons.

14 new backend tests (`ReportServiceTest`, `ReportControllerTest`) locking in the pinned definitions (ledger-sourced profit vs the cached column, `is_test` exclusion, KL day-bucketing, reseller scoping, `paid_at` vs `created_at`). Full backend suite **811/811 green**. `npx tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean on `admin/`. Migration run against the local dev DB (per this repo's own gotcha) — **live-tested in the actual browser** (not just typechecked), which caught a real bug: pre-existing paid orders in the dev DB predated the `paid_at` column and had it `NULL`, surfacing as a "Latest Order: 20691 days ago" (epoch-zero reaching the frontend). Fixed with a one-way backfill migration (`paid_at = created_at` for any already-Paid order missing it) plus a `whereNotNull('paid_at')` defensive floor added to every `ReportService` query — not just the year/month filter.

**Reports redesigned into a tabbed analytics suite, 2026-08-27 (same-week follow-up).** Founder felt the first cut was "tak kemas dan kurang information" and supplied reference mockups (an existing reseller-adjacent product's Reports screen, plus 4 AI-generated layout directions); picked direction **D — Detailed Analytics (tabbed)**. Deliberately *not* copied verbatim: "Refund Rate"/"Chargeback Rate" tiles from one reference don't map to any concept this platform has (ADR-004 — no cash refunds, ever; chargebacks were never tracked) — omitted rather than faked. Donut charts in the references were replaced with horizontal bar charts per the `dataviz` skill's own form guidance ("part-to-whole → bar, not pie/donut").

`ReportService` extended (all still ledger-sourced/`paid_at`-scoped, per the same pinned rules): `avg_order_value` added to `summary()`; new `dailyBreakdown()` (Overview's un-zero-filled, RPT-3-filter-respecting detail table, distinct from the always-last-N-days `dailyTrend()`), `gameBreakdown()` (also serves "Top Performing Games" via a `$limit` param), `paymentMethodBreakdown()`, `resellerBreakdown()`, `orderStatusFunnel()` (delivery-status counts among Paid orders — a health snapshot, not a worklist; that stays at `/admin/orders`). A shared `profitByKey()` helper (renamed from the day-only `profitByDate()`) generalizes the ledger-rollup pattern across date/game/reseller keys. New routes: `GET /reports/{daily-breakdown,top-games,breakdown/games,breakdown/payment-methods,breakdown/resellers,order-status-funnel}`, same role tier as before.

Admin UI: `/admin/reports` is now `Tabs` (new `components/ui/tabs.tsx` PrimeReact wrapper) — **Overview** (5 colored stat cards incl. Avg Order Value, Sales-vs-Owner-Profit trend, Top Performing Games, Detailed Data table), **Sales Analysis** (Sales by Payment Method + Sales by Game bars), **Profit Analysis** (Owner-vs-Reseller-Profit trend + a client-derived Margin % trend — no new backend endpoint needed, computed from the same `dailyTrend()` payload), **Orders** (delivery-status funnel, colored with the dataviz skill's fixed status palette — good/warning/serious/critical, never themed, never reused elsewhere on this page), **Games**/**Payment Methods**/**Resellers** (full breakdown tables). `SalesTrendChart` generalized into a reusable `TrendChart` (1- or 2-series) backing all three trend charts on the page. New `HorizontalBarList` (single-hue ranked bars — dataviz's "sequential is the safe default" for plain magnitude ranking) and `OrderStatusFunnelChart`. Each tab lazy-fetches its own data only once selected (`{activeTab === X && <Tab .../>}`), not all seven on load.

7 new backend tests added for the new endpoints/service methods. Full backend suite **818/818 green**. `npx tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean on `admin/`. Live-tested in the browser across all 7 tabs, both themes — automated clicks on the PrimeReact `Select`/`Tabs`/login-submit controls were unreliable via the browser tool's synthetic pointer events (needed a raw DOM `.click()` via the JS console instead; real user clicks are unaffected, this was purely a test-tooling quirk).

**Zod runtime schema validation added at the storefront API boundary, 2026-08-27 — grilled before coding (see ADR-044).** Closes a gap this repo's own `AGENTS.md` had stated as a rule but never actually built: neither `admin/` nor `storefront/` had any runtime schema check at the API boundary — every request payload was a compile-time-only TypeScript `interface`, every response was a blind `payload as T` cast. Not a security fix (backend already has full `FormRequest` coverage, ORD-9 means money is never trusted from the client regardless) — a client-side UX and contract-drift-detection gap.

`zod` (`^4.4.3`) added as a dependency to both `admin/` and `storefront/`. **Storefront: full rollout**, all 8 `lib/` files that consume `apiFetch`/`apiUpload` (`checkout.ts`, `catalog.ts`, `vouchers.ts`, `track-order.ts`, `hero-slides.ts`, `payment-methods.ts`, `branding.ts`, `seo.ts`) — every hand-written `*Wire` interface replaced with a `z.object()` schema, `z.infer` now the source of truth for the type. New shared `lib/schema-validation.ts` (`parseResponse()`) — response schemas fail soft (log + fall back to the raw payload) rather than throw, since detecting drift must never itself become a new failure mode on the checkout path. `checkout.ts` additionally gained `CheckoutContactSchema` (request-side — validates email/name/phone in `OrderForm.tsx`'s `handleConfirmPayment` before the round trip, mirroring `CreateCheckoutRequest::rules()` exactly) and re-validates the full outgoing payload via `CheckoutPayloadSchema.parse()` right before it's sent. **Admin: dependency added, no schemas written yet** — stays convention-only (same opportunistic trigger as `ADR-038`'s PrimeReact migration), deferred given admin's ~120-function surface (~7-8x storefront's) is too large a lift for one pass.

New backend endpoint `POST /api/client-errors` (`ClientErrorController`, `ReportClientErrorRequest`, `throttle:30,1`) — public, receives `{schema, path, error}` drift reports from either app and `Log::warning`s them, giving the founder backend-log visibility into a mismatch that would otherwise only ever appear in a customer's own browser console. Deliberately narrow — a single log sink for one event type, not a general error-monitoring platform.

3 new backend tests (`ClientErrorControllerTest`). Full backend suite **828/828 green**. `npx tsc --noEmit`/`eslint`/`next build` clean on `storefront/`.

**Admin Dashboard built, 2026-08-27 — DASH-1..6 (§6.2), grilled before coding across four rounds, 27 pinned decisions, recorded as [ADR-045](./adr.md).** The route every login has landed on since day one (`admin/src/app/admin/page.tsx`, sidebar's own "Dashboard" nav entry) was still a placeholder — its own doc comment read "DASH-1..DASH-6 ... come later." Founder chose to grill and build all six requirements in one pass rather than split DASH-1/2 now and DASH-3..6 later.

Same "pin the exact definition before writing the query" discipline Reports (RPT-1..3) already established, applied to landmines specific to this screen: **DASH-1**'s "Refunds Today" directly conflicted with ADR-004 (no cash refunds, ever) — renamed "Vouchers Issued Today," counting only Path B (order-triggered) vouchers, the real compensation-for-a-failed-order signal. Sales/Orders/Profit Today reuse `ReportService`'s own definitions verbatim (ledger-sourced profit, `paid_at`-scoped, `is_test`-excluded) rather than re-deriving them. **DASH-2** derives supplier status from `CircuitBreaker::state()` (built in ADR-019 specifically anticipating this screen, never wired to anything until now) — never a live ping, both for the §9 2-second budget and to avoid the dashboard itself tripping the breaker it displays. "Stuck orders" reuses `ReconcilePendingDeliveriesCommand`'s own existing `delivery_reconciliation` config thresholds rather than inventing a second "stuck" definition. **DASH-3**'s Conversion Funnel is a real cohort (orders created in the last 7 days, measuring that same set's own current Paid/Delivered state) — right-censoring (a day-7 order hasn't had time to convert yet) is an accepted, documented limitation, not engineered around. **DASH-4** reuses `ReportService::gameBreakdown()` unchanged. **DASH-5** is a single-selected-day hourly view (day selector), counted by `created_at` like DASH-3's Created leg. **DASH-6** auto-refreshes only the DASH-2 health section, 60s, plain `setInterval` — `laravel/reverb` isn't installed and standing up broadcast infra for a 60-second single-screen number was judged not worth it.

Cross-cutting: while pinning DASH-3, found `orders` had no `delivered_at` column (unlike `paid_at`) — founder's own call to fix it now rather than scope-creep around it, since the real cost was low (all 3 `DeliveryStatus::Delivered`-transition sites live in one file, `OrderFulfillmentService.php`). New nullable `orders.delivered_at`, stamped at all 3 sites, backfilled for existing Delivered orders (`updated_at` as best-available proxy, flagged as approximation same as `paid_at`'s own backfill). Founder also requested every metric on the page carry a self-documenting `definition` string (generated backend-side, colocated with its calculation) behind a clickable `(i)` button — so a future definition change is self-auditable from the API response alone, without needing to ask an engineer/AI to re-derive and re-explain the current one first.

Backend: `App\Services\Dashboard\DashboardService` (delegates DASH-1 to `ReportService`, owns DASH-2/3/5 via direct `Order::query()` since `ReportService::scopedOrders()` is Paid-only by design), `Admin\DashboardController`, 5 endpoints under the same `admin.role:super_admin,admin` tier as Reports. Admin UI: 4 KPI cards (reusing `StatCard`, wrapped with a new comparison-badge + `InfoTooltip`), System Health (supplier list w/ `Tag` circuit-state badges — ADR-038's Badge→Tag migration — balance, stuck/pending/queue stats), Conversion Funnel, Top Games (reuses `HorizontalBarList` unmodified), Hourly Activity (plain CSS bars, no new charting dependency, matching Reports' own no-library precedent).

32 new backend tests (`DashboardServiceTest`, `DashboardControllerTest`). Full backend suite **860/860 green**. `npx tsc --noEmit`/`eslint`/`next build` clean on `admin/`.

**Customer Analytics (ANL-1..4) built, 2026-08-28 — grilled and pinned as [ADR-049](./adr.md) (7 decisions, 3 rounds) before any code was written.** `App\Services\CustomerAnalytics\CustomerAnalyticsService` groups Paid/non-test orders by `customer_email` (no `Customer` model — ADR-011 untouched), re-expressing `ReportService`'s own Paid/`paid_at`/`final_amount` scoping rule rather than reusing it directly (needs a per-email `GROUP BY`, incompatible with `ReportService::scopedOrders()`'s per-order shape). `CustomerSegment` enum (Vip/Frequent/Dormant/NewCustomer/OneTime), evaluated most-significant-first against each customer's full lifetime order history — the year/month filter only narrows the *displayed* orders_count/total_spent figures and the CSV export, never the segment tag. New `platform_settings.vip_spend_threshold_sen` (default 500000 = RM5,000, the only configurable segment threshold — Frequent/Dormant/New/One-time stay hardcoded constants), wired into the existing Platform Settings tab. `Admin\CustomerAnalyticsController` (summary/customers/export, same `admin.role:super_admin,admin` tier as Reports), new `/admin/customer-analytics` page (PrimeReact-Tailwind — Button/Tag/Select/DataTable, ADR-038), new "Customer Analytics" nav entry.

**Real bug found and fixed during implementation, not assumed away:** this Laravel/Carbon version's `diffInDays()` is *signed* (past→now positive, now→past negative), not absolute like older Carbon — the first-cut `classify()` called it now→past, so every negative diff satisfied both "< 30 days" (falsely tagging every existing customer New) and never ">60 days" (Dormant never triggered). Caught by the service's own test suite before merge (`CustomerAnalyticsServiceTest`), not in production; fixed by always diffing from the stored past timestamp to now. 24 new backend tests (`CustomerAnalyticsServiceTest`, `CustomerAnalyticsControllerTest`, plus 2 in `SettingsControllerTest`); full backend suite **920/920 green**. `npx tsc --noEmit`/`eslint`/`next build` clean on `admin/`. Browser-verified live against real seeded data (6 customers, one per segment) — stat cards, all 5 segment tags, and figures cross-checked against `/admin/reports`' own numbers for the same data (`RM 222.65` avg order value matched exactly on both screens).

**Supplier config guard hardened + Request Logs (MID-10/11, MUI-9) built, 2026-08-28 — two stacked branches, `fix/supplier-config-guard` then `feature/adr-051-request-logs`.** Found while the founder was live-testing a fresh (recently-wiped) local DB: adding a Gamevion supplier row then immediately clicking Refresh Balance crashed raw ("Undefined array key 'bearer_token'") instead of the intended `SupplierNotConfiguredException` — `AppServiceProvider::supplierApiConfig()`'s guard only checked `empty($supplier->api_config)`, which a *partially*-filled config (e.g. only `base_url` saved) passes right through. Fixed by extracting the per-slug field shape (previously a private const only on `SupplierController`) into a shared `App\Services\Supplier\SupplierConfigSchema`, and having the guard check every defined key is actually present, not just "not empty" — now throws a clean, field-specific error ("...is missing required config field(s): bearer_token, api_key..."). Same pass also fixed the "Credentials" badge reading `has_credentials` (true the moment *any* key is saved) instead of a real "fully configured" check — new `is_fully_configured`/`configured_secret_keys` fields, a distinct "Partial" badge state, and per-secret-field accuracy in the Edit form (previously every secret field showed the same supplier-wide flag). Edit form's text fields also gained format-hint placeholders (Base URL scheme/no-trailing-slash, Digiflazz's default `|` separator).

Stacked on that fix: [ADR-051](./adr.md)'s Request Logs, grilled across four rounds before any code — MID-10/11 was the one genuinely-unbuilt piece the same day's Middleware Panel tracker reconciliation (below) flagged. New `supplier_request_logs` table; `SupplierRequestLogger` hooks Guzzle's per-request `on_stats` option (not Laravel's `RequestSending`/`ResponseReceived` events — those carry no way to attach call context, found while building, see ADR-051's build addendum) inside both adapters' `client()` builders, so every real outbound call gets redacted (`SupplierRequestPayloadRedactor` — headers + per-slug secret body keys, e.g. Digiflazz's `sign`) and queued (`LogSupplierRequestJob`, its own `supplier-request-logs` Horizon queue) before ever touching the DB. Circuit-breaker-open skips get a synthetic row too (`CircuitBreakingSupplierAdapter::guarded()`). Split retention (`PruneSupplierRequestLogsCommand`: 30 days default, 7 for `validatePlayer`, matching `player_validations`/ADR-021's own PII window). New `/middleware/request-logs` screen (PrimeReact-Tailwind) — filter row + table + detail modal, nav link un-greyed.

**Real gap found and fixed before this was considered done:** Horizon's supervisors (`config/horizon.php`) list their queues explicitly — the new `supplier-request-logs` queue had no supervisor, so dispatched jobs would have sat forever unprocessed with no error at all. Caught only by trying to verify a real log row live (a foreground `queue:work --queue=supplier-request-logs --stop-when-empty` drain proved the write path before Horizon was updated/restarted) — fixed with a new `supervisor-supplier-request-logs` in `defaults` + both `environments` entries. General lesson recorded in ADR-051's addendum: a new queue name needs its own Horizon supervisor, the same way a new migration needs a local `php artisan migrate` — nothing enforces it at write time.

17 new backend tests across both branches; full backend suite **948/948 green**. `npx tsc --noEmit`/`eslint`/`next build` clean on `admin/`. Browser-verified live end-to-end on the real local Gamevion connection: Refresh Balance → real HTTP call → redacted+queued → drained → row visible in Request Logs → detail modal confirms `Authorization`/`X-API-KEY` show `[REDACTED]`, non-secret response body shown in full.
