**24/7 resilience & security hardening, round 2 (ADR-019, 2026-07-27):** ADR-014's baseline (queue/retry/indexing/caching/rate-limiting/observability) is confirmed fully shipped, not just designed. Cross-cutting gaps found on re-audit, not tied to one feature area above: no rate limit on login (Auth row's "MFA not started" note also still applies — the columns exist but are unchecked, not truly "not started" at the schema level), `orders.created_at`/`whereDate()` indexing gaps and 7 unverified FK indexes (Orders/Money-core rows), no Xendit payment idempotency key (Payment Gateway row), Image Gallery's disk is hardcoded not config-driven (corrects that row's "costs a disk-config change, not a rewrite" claim — true only once the hardcoding is fixed), and no queue-worker/Redis/S3 provisioned anywhere in `docker-compose.yml` (blocks running more than one app instance, not a feature-level gap). **Self-review before commit found one more, more significant gap the audit itself missed: a per-supplier circuit breaker is an explicit MVP requirement (`foundation-security.md` §6, DASH-2) with zero implementation** — the existing `TransientFailureRetryPolicy` retries within a request, it doesn't stop attempting calls to a supplier having a sustained outage the way a circuit breaker does. Tracked as its own item in ADR-019's addendum, not folded into the fix-now list silently. None of this blocks any area above from continuing — see ADR-019 (and its addendum) for the fix-now vs. deferred-until-host-chosen vs. deferred-until-real-traffic sequencing.

**ADR-019's fix-now list closed out, 2026-07-29** — every item shipped except MFA (explicitly descoped, founder's own call — see the Auth row above and ADR-019's newest addendum). The deferred-until-host-chosen and deferred-until-real-traffic items are unaffected — those still wait on ADR-020's actual provisioning, tracked there, not here.

~~**➡️ NEXT SESSION STARTS HERE (2026-07-29 pointer).** With ADR-019's fix-now list closed and ADR-020 designed (not yet built), real candidates in rough priority order: (1) **ADR-020 Phase 1**...; (2) **Blacklist/Fraud (FRAUD-1..4)**...~~ **Item 2 done, same day (new session) — see [ADR-007's shipped addendum](./adr.md) and the Blacklist/Fraud row above.**

**Blacklist/Fraud (FRAUD-1..4) shipped, 2026-07-29 (new session) — ADR-007's ~6-week-outstanding decision finally built:** `blacklist_entries` (type/value/reason/created_by/is_active — soft-deactivate, never hard-deleted) + `blacklist_hits` (insert-only audit trail, since a blocked attempt never creates an `Order`). `App\Services\Fraud\BlacklistService::check()` matches player_id/email/phone against active entries; wired into a new `CheckoutController::assertNotBlacklisted()`, called right after player-ID validation, before Reseller/gateway resolution (§7.1 step 5's exact ordering). FRAUD-4's velocity guard (`CheckoutVelocityGuard`) is a genuinely separate mechanism from ADR-014's general `throttle:10,1` — it counts only blacklist-triggered rejections per IP (3 per 10 minutes, default), the carding-probing signal specifically, not general request volume. Both paths reject with the identical generic message ("This order cannot be processed.") so a real fraudster can't distinguish blacklisted from rate-limited from the response alone. Admin screen `/admin/blacklist` mirrors the Vouchers page's existing stat-cards/table/create-modal structure, plus a blocked-attempt history modal for FRAUD-3. 44 new tests, full backend suite 440 green, real-MySQL migration verified clean. Full detail in [ADR-007's shipped addendum](./adr.md) — not duplicated here.

**Founder-testing follow-up, same day:** live-testing `/admin/blacklist` against the local dev server hit `SQLSTATE[HY000]: no such table: blacklist_entries` — not a code bug. The new migrations had only run against the test suites' own databases (sqlite `:memory:`, the dockerized MySQL for concurrency tests), never against `backend/database/database.sqlite`, the actual local dev DB `composer run dev`/Herd serves against. `php artisan migrate:status` confirmed 3 pending migrations (this feature's two tables + the earlier `orders.created_at` index); `php artisan migrate` against the local dev DB resolved it immediately, no data loss (additive-only migrations). Now a documented, named gotcha — see `AGENTS.md`'s "Second known gotcha."

**➡️ NEXT SESSION STARTS HERE (2026-07-29 pointer, updated same day after Blacklist/Fraud shipped).** Remaining candidates, rough priority order: (1) **ADR-020 Phase 1** — Dockerfiles, `docker-compose.prod.yml`, GitHub Actions CI/CD, all buildable and provable without the droplet existing yet; (2) **Playwright E2E** — ADR-020 decision #9's own explicit "immediate next priority," and this is a money-moving system with currently zero UI-regression coverage; ~~(3) **checkout-level idempotency key** — the real gap this session's Xendit research surfaced (a retried `POST /api/checkout` mints a new `order_number` each time), needs a `storefront/`-touching request-contract decision, not decided this session;~~ **item 3 done, later same day — see [ADR-019's newest addendum](./adr.md) and the entry below**; (4) a full migration-file sweep for the MySQL 64-char identifier-length pattern this session found twice by accident — worth checking deliberately, not by accident a third time, before ADR-020's first real production deploy. None of these block each other.

**Checkout-level idempotency key shipped, 2026-07-29 (later same day) — closes the one gap ADR-019's fix-now list left open.** Surfaced as a pre-requisite while planning the platform's first deliberate real end-to-end purchase-workflow proof (Xendit test-mode + Gamevion **production**, since Gamevion's own sandbox is confirmed broken — see ADR-006's addendums): a network hiccup during that real-money test is exactly when a double-order is most likely, so this known gap closed first, grilled via `/mattpocock-skills:grilling` before implementation. New `orders.checkout_idempotency_key` (nullable, **unique**) column, stamped at Order creation (not after payment succeeds) so a genuine race between two near-simultaneous requests fails one at the DB layer, not just an app-level check. Storefront (`OrderForm.tsx`) generates one `crypto.randomUUID()` per Review Modal open, reused across any resubmit of that same attempt. `CheckoutController::store()` now looks up the Order by key **before** re-running player-validation/blacklist/velocity checks, and branches: not found → normal `initiate()`; found with `payment_ref` set → pure idempotent replay (`200`, no new Order, no second gateway call); found with `payment_ref` still null (previous attempt's gateway call failed) → new `CheckoutService::resume()` retries only the payment leg against the same Order, reusing its own snapshotted pricing (ORD-9). Full detail — including the accepted residual race-window limitation — in [ADR-019's newest addendum](./adr.md), not duplicated here. 6 new `CheckoutControllerTest` cases + 3 new `CheckoutServiceTest` cases; full backend suite **447/447 green** (up from 396). `npx tsc --noEmit`/`next build`/`eslint` clean on `storefront/`. Migration run against the real local dev DB (sqlite), verified via `PRAGMA index_list`.

**First deliberate real end-to-end purchase-workflow test, 2026-07-29 (same day, right after the idempotency fix) — payment half fully proven live, delivery half blocked on Gamevion production, not us.** Setup: real storefront checkout, `XENDIT_SECRET_KEY` in real test-mode, `GAMEVION_SANDBOX=false` (production — a deliberate one-off per ADR-006's own "not recommended casually" framing, planned and grilled first), a Cloudflare quick tunnel exposing the local backend's webhook route to the real internet for the first time ever in this project (every prior session only simulated the webhook). Three real, previously-unknown bugs found and fixed live, all blocking a real browser checkout from working at all before today:
1. **CORS never allowed `storefront/`'s origin** — `config/cors.php` was written for `admin/` only (its own doc comment said so explicitly) before `storefront/` existed and started calling the API directly from the browser. Every storefront API call silently failed with a browser-side "failed to fetch," invisible in Laravel's own logs since the request technically completed — only visible via a real browser's Network tab or `javascript_tool`-driven `fetch()` from the actual page context. Fixed: `allowed_origins` now merges `ADMIN_URL` and a new `STOREFRONT_URL` env (defaults `http://localhost:3000`/`:3001` — the real common case when both dev servers run together via `scripts/dev.sh`, `admin/` claiming `:3000` first).
2. **`channel_properties` never sent `success_return_url`/`failure_return_url`** — `OrderForm.tsx` never included them; Xendit's real Payment Request v3 API rejects an FPX channel outright without them (`API_VALIDATION_ERROR`). Fixed: storefront now sends both, pointed at `/track-order` (no per-order URL needed — `/track-order` already does exactly what's needed for either outcome).
3. **`payment_actions` shape mismatch** — `extractCheckoutRedirectUrl()` expected a flat `{desktop_web_checkout_url: ...}` object; Xendit's real v3 API actually returns an `actions` **array** of `{type, descriptor, value}` objects for a redirect-based channel like FPX. No redirect ever fired — confirmed live by fetching the real payment request back from Xendit directly (`GET /v3/payment_requests/{id}`) and comparing shapes. Fixed: `extractCheckoutRedirectUrl()` now reads the real array shape (`descriptor: "WEB_URL"`), keeping the old flat-object shape as a fallback for any channel/gateway that might still use it.

With all three fixed, a real checkout went all the way through the real Xendit mock-bank redirect flow (login → confirm) **twice**, and both times the real webhook was delivered over the real tunnel and `payment_status` correctly reached `paid` — the payment half of this pipeline is now proven end-to-end for the first time, not simulated. Delivery hit two separate real Gamevion findings (missing-phone 404, then a genuine Gamevion-side production `500` reproduced independently 3 times with fresh reference numbers/different products) — full diagnostic detail in [ADR-006's newest addendum](./adr.md), not duplicated here. No real money was lost in either failure — Gamevion rejects before deducting balance.

~~**➡️ NEXT SESSION STARTS HERE (2026-07-29 pointer, updated same day after the live end-to-end test).** (1) Waiting on Gamevion support's reply re: the production `500`...~~ **Resolved, same day — Gamevion support replied fast and pointed at a missing `callback_url` field, our own bug, not theirs. **`customer_phone` made required, 2026-07-30 (new session) — closes item 1 of the pointer above.** `CreateCheckoutRequest`'s `customer_phone` rule changed `nullable` → `required` (was flagged live 2026-07-29: Gamevion's real order endpoint rejects delivery with `404 "Phone number required"` when it's missing, but the field was only ever checked at the delivery step, after payment). Storefront: `CheckoutPayload.customer_phone` (`storefront/src/lib/checkout.ts`) is no longer optional; `OrderForm.tsx` sends the raw value instead of collapsing an empty string to `undefined`; `ReviewModal.tsx`'s phone field dropped its "(optional)" label, gained `canConfirm` gating (blank phone now blocks "Confirm & Pay" the same way blank email/name already did) and a `required` HTML attribute. 1 new `CheckoutControllerTest` case (`test_rejects_checkout_without_a_customer_phone`); full backend suite **449/449 green**. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on `storefront/`. `GamevionAdapter::normalizePhone()` left untouched — it already handled a null phone defensively, no behavior change needed there now that the caller guarantees non-null.

**Post-payment redirect corrected to the customer's own order, 2026-07-29 (new session) — supersedes finding #2 in the live end-to-end test entry above.** Founder caught it directly: after a real payment, the customer landed on the general `/track-order` search form instead of straight onto their own order's status. That earlier fix was deliberate at the time — the storefront genuinely can't know `order_number` when it builds the checkout request, since the `Order` doesn't exist yet — but the reasoning stopped one step too early: the **backend** does know it, from the moment `CheckoutService::initiate()` creates the `Order`, which happens *before* `requestPayment()` ever calls Xendit. Fixed there instead of in the storefront: `CheckoutService::requestPayment()` now overwrites whatever `success_return_url`/`failure_return_url` the storefront sent with the real `{STOREFRONT_URL}/order/status/{order_number}`, for both the fresh-checkout (`initiate()`) and resumed-checkout (`resume()`) paths, since both funnel through this one method. New `config('services.storefront.url')` (first entry of the existing comma-separated `STOREFRONT_URL` env `cors.php` already reads). `OrderForm.tsx`'s `/track-order` value is now only ever a fallback that should never actually reach Xendit unmodified — comment updated to say so. 1 new `CheckoutServiceTest` case (asserts the gateway receives the per-order URL, not the storefront's generic one); full backend suite 448/448 green.

**`foundation-security.md` checklist reconciled against real code, 2026-07-30 (new session).** Founder asked for the checklist to be re-verified item-by-item against actual code, not trusted as written. 13 items flipped to `[x]` with file-level evidence (RBAC enforcement, maker-checker, ledger-as-SUM, no-cash-refund, `reference_number` pre-supplier-call, webhook idempotency/signature, no raw card data, xenPlatform not wired, supplier-adapter-only access, `supports_validation` per-mapping, proxy seam, no raw credentials in the auth/checkout logs actually spot-checked). 5 left `[ ]` with a reason each: MFA (AUTH-7, deliberately descoped), Reseller impersonation (RES-4, correctly Phase 2), Developer Tools masking (DEV-2, screen doesn't exist to verify), Request Logs redaction (MID-10, no logs table exists), and two **genuinely open gaps this pass confirmed, not previously this explicit**: the reconciliation job (ORD-10/PAY-3) and `player_validations` PII retention having no TTL/cleanup anywhere. Both directly fed into the re-prioritization below.

**Payment reconciliation job (PAY-3) shipped, 2026-07-30 (new session) — closes item 1 of the pointer below.** `App\Console\Commands\Payment\ReconcilePendingPaymentsCommand` (`app:reconcile-pending-payments`), scheduled every 15 minutes via the same inert-until-real-cron `Schedule::call()` pattern as Price Sync (`routes/console.php`) — a no-op locally today, activates for free once a real OS cron exists on a deployed host. Queries orders `payment_status=pending`, `created_at` older than `payment_reconciliation.pending_after_minutes` (config default 30), with a non-null `payment_ref`; for each, calls `PaymentGateway::getPayment()` and branches on the **raw** Xendit status (confirmed against `docs.xendit.co`: `SUCCEEDED`/`EXPIRED`/`FAILED`/`CANCELED` are real terminal statuses) — `SUCCEEDED` recovers via the existing `FulfillOrderJob`/`OrderFulfillmentService::fulfill()` path (with the same PAY-2 duplicate-processing guard `XenditWebhookController` already has), a terminal failure marks the order `failed`, anything still ambiguous past `payment_reconciliation.flag_after_hours` (config default 24) is logged for review — never auto-decided — and left exactly as-is otherwise. One deliberate deviation from ADR-021's literal wording, recorded here since it's a real finding: the job resolves the gateway via the **default `PaymentGateway` binding**, not `PaymentGatewayFactory` — `Order` never actually persists the `channel_code` a per-channel factory lookup would need (only the coarser `payment_method` category), so there's no stored value to feed it. `XenditWebhookController` already makes this exact same simplification for the same reason; only one gateway (`'xendit'`) is bound today, so nothing behaves differently in practice — flagged as a joint revisit if a second gateway is ever added, not a new gap this job introduces. New `/admin/orders?status=awaiting_payment` filter (`Admin\OrderController::index()`) closes the visibility gap the 2026-07-30 audit found — same 30-minute window the job itself acts on — plus a matching tab in the admin Orders screen. 9 new `ReconcilePendingPaymentsCommandTest` cases + 1 new `OrderControllerTest` case; full backend suite **459/459 green**. `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean on `admin/`.

**Hardening batch shipped, 2026-07-30 (same session, later) — closes item 2 of the pointer below.** MySQL 64-char sweep run read-only against real code first: every `database/migrations/*.php` file's `->unique(...)`/`->index(...)` call (41 files) plus every `foreignId()->constrained()` implicit FK name, computed against Laravel's real naming formula (`Blueprint::createIndexName()`). **Nothing else exceeds 64 characters today** — closest is `player_region_mappings`'s own FK to `player_validator_profiles` at 58 chars. No new migration fix needed; deliberately didn't build a bespoke static-analysis test for this (a new, fragile mechanism for a problem ADR-020 Phase 1's CI — the very next item — already catches for real via the concurrency suite against real MySQL). Instead, `backend/CLAUDE.md`'s existing "Migrations:" note now also names the 64-char limit explicitly, closing the gap until Phase 1's CI lands. **`player_validations` pruning**: new `App\Console\Commands\PlayerValidation\PrunePlayerValidationsCommand` (`app:prune-player-validations`), scheduled daily (same inert-until-real-cron pattern), deletes rows where `validated_at` is older than `config('services.player_validation.retention_days')` (default 7, per ADR-021's own grilled number). Full detail in [ADR-021's newest addendum](./adr.md). 3 new test cases; full backend suite **462/462 green**.

**ADR-020 Phase 1 shipped (Docker + CI, buildable without the droplet), 2026-07-30 (same session, later) — closes item 3 of the pointer below.** Prerequisite fixed first: no job actually declared a queue name despite ADR-020 decision #5 naming `queue-worker-orders`/`queue-worker-pricesync` — fixed via `$this->onQueue(...)` in each job's constructor (`FulfillOrderJob`/`ResendOrderDeliveryJob` → `orders`, `SyncSupplierPricesJob` → `price-sync`; a redeclared `public $queue` property, tried first, is rejected by PHP as incompatible with `Queueable`'s own property of the same name — real error, hit and fixed live). Then built and **locally verified** (not just written): `backend/Dockerfile`, `admin/Dockerfile`, `storefront/Dockerfile` (both Next.js apps needed `output: "standalone"` added, neither had it), `backend/docker/nginx/default.conf`, `docker-compose.prod.yml` (repo root, 7 services, no `mysql`/Spaces — those stay off-box), `predis/predis` added (no Redis PHP client existed at all), and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (backend sqlite + concurrency-against-real-MySQL + admin/storefront tsc/build/eslint, then a build-and-push job — the SSH-deploy job deliberately not written yet, needs a real droplet). Three real bugs found only by actually building/running the stack, not by reading the Dockerfile: a missing `oniguruma-dev` Alpine package for `mbstring`; `composer.lock` actually requiring PHP ≥8.4.1 despite `composer.json`'s stale `^8.3` (switched the image/CI to 8.4, `composer.json` itself left uncorrected — a small flagged follow-up); and stale local `bootstrap/cache/*.php` (compiled with dev deps) getting copied into the image and clobbering its own fresh `--no-dev` regeneration, surfacing as a missing Pail provider class — fixed via `.dockerignore`. After both fixes, `GET /api/health` through the real `nginx`→`php-fpm` stack returned a correct `503 degraded` (no real `.env`/DB wired into this local check, by design) — proving the exact mechanism a future CI rollback step depends on. Full detail in [ADR-020's newest addendum](./adr.md). Still genuinely not done: the SSH-deploy job, the actual droplet/Managed MySQL/Registry/secret, and git-remote creation.

**`payment_methods` seeded fee rates corrected against Xendit's real published rate card, 2026-07-30 (new session, prompted by the payment-gateway-swap discussion below).** While researching ChipIn Asia/HitPay as candidate second gateways, checked whether the founder's "Xendit's fees are high" concern (ADR-001's 2026-07-25 addendum) was reflected accurately in `payment_methods`' seeded defaults — it wasn't. `PaymentMethodSeeder`'s numbers were the old deleted `config/checkout.php` stopgap's guessed figures carried forward, never cross-checked against Xendit's own rate card. Fetched `xendit.co/en/pricing` live and found several channels materially understated: e-wallets were blanket-seeded at 1.9% when real rates range 1.80%–2.50% per channel (ShopeePay/WeChat Pay are actually 2.50%, not 1.9%); all FPX codes (including `_BUSINESS` variants) were seeded at the personal RM2.10 flat fee, when business FPX is really RM2.90. Fixed both in `PaymentMethodSeeder`, with per-channel citations in its own doc comment. Two gaps corrected numbers can't close, documented rather than guessed at: `CARDS` is one Xendit channel code spanning three real rate tiers (debit 1.90%/credit 2.00%/international 3.80%, decided by the card presented, not selectable in advance) — kept at the debit/floor rate; `AMBANK_VIRTUAL_ACCOUNT` stays at 0%/RM0 (still deliberately unconfirmed) since Xendit's real 0.50%-with-RM1.00-minimum has no minimum-floor concept in the current schema. New `PaymentMethodSeederTest::test_seeds_fee_rates_matching_xendits_published_rate_card()` locks in the corrected figures so they can't silently drift back to guessed placeholders. Full backend suite 466/466 green. **Note:** this only fixes the seeder's *defaults* — any `payment_methods` row already seeded in a running dev/production DB before this fix keeps its old value until an admin re-saves its fee via the existing Payment Methods UI (`firstOrCreate` doesn't touch existing rows); not retroactively patched here, deliberately, since a live fee value should be a reviewed admin action, not a silent migration-time overwrite.

**Multi-gateway payment strategy decided (design only) — CHIP for Malaysia-local, Xendit retained for international + split-payment, HitPay evaluated and not adopted — 2026-07-30 (same session, later), grilled via `/mattpocock-skills:grilling`.** Prompted by the fee-correction entry above and the founder's confirmation that real third-party reseller onboarding is now genuinely near-term (making split-payment/payout capability a live requirement, not Phase 2-hypothetical). Full primary-source research on CHIP and HitPay captured in `docs/research-payment-gateways-2026-07-30.md`; full grilled decision in **[ADR-022](./adr.md)**. Summary: `ledger_entries`/`ledger_accounts` (ADR-002) already satisfies the founder's accounting-safety need, so "split-payment capability" was reframed from "needs xenPlatform specifically" to "needs a payout API + reseller never sees the processor" — which both Xendit's `xenPlatform` OWNED and CHIP Send can satisfy. Decision: adopt **CHIP** for Malaysia-local channels (FPX first — its flat RM1 fee is an unconditional per-transaction saving over Xendit's own flat RM2.10/RM2.90, verified flat-vs-flat, not a percentage assumption); **retain Xendit unchanged** for both international customer-facing acceptance (confirmed direct support for every method the founder named — Indonesia GoPay/DANA/OVO/BCA, Philippines GCash/Maya, Singapore PayNow — vs HitPay's confirmed Indonesia gap) and split-payment (Xendit's OWNED shape confirmed safe; HitPay's "Platforms" feature's OWNED-vs-MANAGED shape is unverified, with an OAuth-based sub-merchant integration detail found in HitPay's own docs actively suggesting the disqualifying MANAGED shape); **HitPay not adopted in any role** this round. Nothing built yet — this session is decision-only. Concrete next actions (see ADR-022 decision #5): start CHIP merchant registration (business info + bank details, ~1-3 business day approval), and in parallel build the `ChipGateway` adapter foundation from CHIP's published OpenAPI spec (`Http::fake()`-based tests, no live account needed for this part) — but do not flip any `payment_methods.gateway` row to `'chip'` live, or consider the adapter "verified," until a new `app:chip-smoke-test` command (mirroring the existing Xendit/Gamevion smoke-test commands) proves it end-to-end against a real CHIP account, per this project's own twice-bitten "docs alone aren't enough" history (Xendit's customer-object gotcha, Gamevion's telp-format bug).

**➡️ NEXT SESSION STARTS HERE (2026-07-30 pointer, updated same day after the payment-gateway/backlog-audit discussion) — see [ADR-021](./adr.md)/[ADR-022](./adr.md) for the full grilled decisions.** Founder-set order, explicit this time, after a full pending-ADR audit surfaced one genuinely stale item:

Nothing new to decide, straight to implementation.

Do not flip `payment_methods.gateway` to `'chip'` live or call it "verified" until a real `app:chip-smoke-test` command proves it end-to-end — see ADR-022 decision #5.
3. **Everything else, unordered among themselves** — Playwright E2E (ADR-020 decision #9's own "immediate next priority," zero UI-regression coverage today); ADR-020's remaining provisioning (SSH-deploy CI job, real droplet/Managed MySQL/Registry, git-remote creation — all need an explicit go-ahead); ADR-017 decision #9 (cross-game package swap, deliberately deferred, revisit only if a real need surfaces).

None of these block each other structurally, but 1 → 2 → 3 is the founder's explicit intended order this time, not left open like the previous pointer's item 4/5.

**ADR-018 (Sandbox Test Orders) shipped, 2026-07-30/31 (new session, closes item 1 of the pointer above).** Built exactly to the 8 decisions recorded 2026-07-27 — no scope changes, no re-grilling needed. `orders.is_test` (boolean, default `false`); `App\Services\Supplier\FakeSupplierAdapter` (zero network calls, returns a canned `SANDBOX-*` ref on simulated success or the admin's chosen error_code/error_message on simulated failure); `Middleware\SandboxOrderController` (index/store/show/resend/destroy/destroyAll, every query unconditionally `is_test = true`); `Admin\OrderController` gained the mirror-image permanent `is_test = false` scope on **all four** of its actions (`index`/`show`/`retryDelivery`/`resend`) — the latter two weren't explicitly named in ADR-018's decision #2 wording but are directly implied by it (a sandbox order id must never reach the real, queued `GamevionAdapter` path), found and closed during implementation, not left as a gap. `OrderFulfillmentService::creditProfit()` gained the one-line `is_test` guard. Frontend: `OrderDetailCards`/`DeliveryLogsTable` extracted from `/admin/orders` into shared components (decision #8), `ResendDeliveryModal` gained a `sandbox` prop (outcome picker), new `/middleware/sandbox` page + `CreateSandboxOrderModal` + `lib/sandboxOrders.ts`, new "Sandbox" nav entry. 19 new backend tests, full suite **483/483 green**; `tsc`/`next build` clean. Verified live twice — direct API calls against the real local Herd backend (create → resend-success → resend-failure → delete, confirmed zero `LedgerEntry` rows and a real `404` from every `Admin\OrderController` action against a sandbox order id) and the actual browser UI end-to-end (create → simulate failure showing the typed error in Supplier response → simulate success reaching `delivered` with a `SANDBOX-*` ref and the Resend button correctly disappearing → Delivery Logs showing both attempts → delete), including confirming a game with player-ID validation enabled correctly blocks a sandbox resend with the same message the real flow gives (`OrderResendService`'s validation logic runs completely unchanged, per decision #6). Full detail in [ADR-018's shipped addendum](./adr.md).

~~**➡️ NEXT SESSION STARTS HERE (2026-07-30 pointer, updated same day after the payment-gateway/backlog-audit discussion).** Founder-set order...~~ **Item 1 done, same session (new day's work) — see the entry directly above.**

~~**➡️ NEXT SESSION STARTS HERE (2026-07-31 pointer, updated after ADR-018 shipped).** Founder-set order unchanged for the rest: (1) **CHIP adapter foundation (ADR-022)**...~~ **Item 1 re-sequenced into 7 concrete sub-steps, 2026-08-03 (new session) — see [ADR-022's newest addendum](./adr.md) and the pointer below.**

**➡️ NEXT SESSION STARTS HERE (2026-08-03 pointer, updated after grilling the CHIP-adapter build plan).** Planning the `ChipGateway` build surfaced a real latent bug (`ReconcilePendingPaymentsCommand` would silently misroute a CHIP-paid order to `XenditGateway` once CHIP exists) plus a payment-method-identity gap and a storefront live-wiring gap — all grilled and decided, full detail in [ADR-022's newest addendum](./adr.md), nothing built yet. Founder-set build order, explicit:

1. **`orders.payment_gateway`/`channel_code` snapshot columns + `ReconcilePendingPaymentsCommand` fix** — ship and fully test standalone, before any `ChipGateway` code (it's a real fix to today's Xendit-only reconciliation path, not CHIP-specific).
2. **`payment_methods.method_key` + `PaymentMethodController::updateStatus()` mutual-exclusivity guard** — prevents two rows representing the same real-world payment method (e.g. TnG via two different gateways) from both being active at once.
3. **Public payment-methods endpoint + storefront wiring** — replaces `storefront/src/lib/placeholder-data.ts`'s hardcoded `PLACEHOLDER_PAYMENT_CHANNELS` (confirmed not live today, for Xendit either) with a real gateway-agnostic endpoint; delete the placeholder once wired.
4. **`ChipGateway`** (`Http::fake()`-based tests, from CHIP's OpenAPI spec) + config/env scaffolding + `payment-gateway.chip` container binding.
5. **`ChipWebhookController`** (RSA PKCS#1 v1.5 signature verification, per ADR-022 decision #3).
6. **`PaymentMethodSeeder`** — add CHIP FPX row(s), `is_active=false`.
7. **`app:chip-smoke-test`** — do not flip any `payment_methods.gateway` row to `'chip'` live or call the adapter "verified" until this proves it end-to-end (ADR-022 decision #5, unchanged).

None of these block **Playwright E2E** (ADR-020 decision #9's "immediate next priority," zero UI-regression coverage today), ADR-020's remaining provisioning (SSH-deploy CI job, real droplet/Managed MySQL/Registry — needs explicit go-ahead), or ADR-017 decision #9 (cross-game package swap, deliberately deferred) — those remain open, unordered among themselves, whenever this list is done. Founder's longer-term **country-based gateway routing** (Malaysia→CHIP, other countries→Xendit, automatic) is explicitly out of scope until multi-currency/multi-country storefront architecture is planned — see ADR-022's addendum, not decided or built here.

**Item 1 shipped, 2026-08-03 (same session) — `orders.payment_gateway`/`channel_code` snapshot + `ReconcilePendingPaymentsCommand` fix, built test-first (`/mattpocock-skills:tdd`).** New migration adds both nullable columns, backfilling `payment_gateway='xendit'` for every existing row with a `payment_ref` (2 real orders, confirmed via tinker against the real local dev DB after `php artisan migrate` — the documented "migrations don't auto-run against the local dev DB" gotcha applied again, caught and handled, not hit blind). `CheckoutRequest` gained a `paymentGateway` field; `CheckoutController::store()` passes the already-resolved `PaymentMethod`'s `gateway` column through; `CheckoutService::initiate()` stamps both columns onto the `Order` at creation. `ReconcilePendingPaymentsCommand` now takes `PaymentGatewayFactory` instead of a single injected `PaymentGateway`, resolving `PaymentGatewayFactory::make($order->payment_gateway)` per order inside the loop — a `payment_gateway === null` order is skipped and `Log::error()`'d (distinct from `flagIfStale()`'s pre-existing `Log::warning()`) rather than guessed at a default gateway. New tests: `CheckoutServiceTest`/`CheckoutControllerTest` each gained a stamping-assertion case; `ReconcilePendingPaymentsCommandTest` gained a two-orders-two-gateways routing case and a no-payment_gateway skip+log case, plus every existing case's fake-gateway binding moved from the plain `PaymentGateway::class` default to the same `payment-gateway.<name>` container key `PaymentGatewayFactory` actually resolves through (matching `CheckoutControllerTest`'s own established convention). Full backend suite **488/488 green** (up from 483). Next: item 2 (`payment_methods.method_key` + mutual-exclusivity guard).

**Item 2 shipped, 2026-08-03 (same session) — `payment_methods.method_key` + `PaymentMethodController::updateStatus()` mutual-exclusivity guard, built test-first.** One open design nuance surfaced first and was deliberately deferred rather than guessed: Xendit exposes FPX as 39 distinct per-bank rows while CHIP's own FPX request shape (generic redirect vs. per-bank-selectable) isn't confirmed yet — pending `ChipGateway` actually being built from CHIP's OpenAPI spec (item 4). So this step ships the general mechanism only; how CHIP's future FPX row(s) should align their `method_key` against the 39 existing Xendit bank rows is left for item 6, once CHIP's real request shape is known, not decided blind here. New nullable `payment_methods.method_key` column, backfilled from each existing row's own lowercased `channel_code` (46 rows, all distinct — confirmed via tinker against the real local dev DB after `php artisan migrate`, no two rows represent the same real-world method yet since only Xendit has ever been seeded). `PaymentMethodController::updateStatus()` gained `assertMethodKeyNotActiveElsewhere()`: activating a row now throws a `422` if another row sharing its `method_key` is already `is_active` (deactivating is always allowed). `PaymentMethodSeeder::upsert()` gained an optional `$methodKey` parameter (defaults to lowercased `channel_code`) so future non-default-key rows (e.g. a CHIP row deliberately aligned to an existing Xendit method_key) have a way in. New tests: `PaymentMethodControllerTest` gained block/allow/deactivate-still-allowed cases for the guard; `PaymentMethodSeederTest` gained a distinct-method_key-per-row assertion. Full backend suite **492/492 green**. Next: item 3 (public payment-methods endpoint + storefront wiring).

**Item 3 shipped, 2026-08-03 (same session) — public payment-methods endpoint + storefront wiring, built test-first.** New `App\Http\Controllers\PaymentMethodCatalogController` (deliberately separate from the admin-only `Middleware\PaymentMethodController`, same reasoning `CatalogController`'s own doc comment gives for staying separate from `GameController`) — `GET /api/catalog/payment-methods`, public/no-auth, 60s-TTL cached same as `CatalogController`/`HeroSlideController`, returning only `channel_code`/`label`/`category` for `is_active=true` rows — `gateway`/`method_key` never serialized here, confirmed by a dedicated test asserting the exact key set. `Middleware\PaymentMethodController::updateStatus()` now also calls `PaymentMethodCatalogController::forgetCache()` (mirroring `GameController` → `CatalogController::forgetIndexCache()`) — `updateFee()`/`test()` don't, since neither changes `is_active`. Storefront: new `lib/payment-methods.ts` (`listPaymentChannels()`, same wire-to-UI-shape translation pattern as `lib/catalog.ts`), fetched server-side in `order/[slug]/page.tsx` and passed into `OrderForm` as a `paymentChannels` prop, replacing the import of `placeholder-data.ts`'s hardcoded `PLACEHOLDER_PAYMENT_CHANNELS` — which is now deleted (confirmed via grep it had no other consumers before removal). This closes a pre-existing gap for Xendit too, not just enabling CHIP: before this, no admin `/middleware/payment-methods` change had ever reached a real customer's checkout screen. 5 new backend tests (`PaymentMethodCatalogControllerTest`); full backend suite **497/497 green**. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (on the touched files — one pre-existing, unrelated lint failure in `TrackOrderClient.tsx` confirmed via `git status` to predate this session, not introduced here), and `next build` all clean on `storefront/`. Next: item 4 (`ChipGateway` adapter).

**Item 4 shipped, 2026-08-03 (same session) — `ChipGateway` adapter, built from CHIP Collect's real OpenAPI spec, plus two foundational `PaymentGateway` fixes found while building it.** Two real gaps surfaced during design, both grilled with the founder before touching code, since both widen the stable `PaymentGateway` seam (not just add a new implementation):

1. **`verifyWebhookSignature()` widened from `(string $providedToken): bool` to `(Request $request): bool`.** CHIP's RSA PKCS#1 v1.5 verification structurally needs the raw request body + the `X-Signature` header, which a single pre-extracted string could never carry — unlike a two-string-parameter fix (rejected as less solid — it would need widening again for a third gateway's own scheme, and leaves an unused parameter on `XenditGateway`), passing the whole `Request` means each adapter reads only what it needs and the interface never needs to change again, matching `SupplierAdapter`'s self-contained-adapter discipline (ADR-006). `XenditGateway`/`XenditWebhookController` updated to match (`hash_equals` now reads the header itself); every fake `PaymentGateway` implementation across the test suite (7 files) updated to the new signature — pure mechanical change, no behavior difference for Xendit.
2. **`PaymentResponse` gained a typed `?PaymentStatus $status` field**, separate from the existing untyped `data` bag. Found while designing `ChipGateway::getPayment()`: `ReconcilePendingPaymentsCommand` was matching `$payment->data['status']` against Xendit's raw strings (`'SUCCEEDED'`/`'EXPIRED'`/...) directly — harmless while only Xendit existed, but CHIP's own raw vocabulary (`'paid'`/`'error'`/`'cancelled'`) would silently fail to match, misclassifying a real paid CHIP order as ambiguous. Fixed at the type level, not by convention: every `createPayment()`/`getPayment()` implementation must now supply a real `PaymentStatus` enum case (PHP's type system enforces this, unlike a string convention a future adapter author could forget) — `XenditGateway` gained `mapPaymentRequestStatus()` (distinct from the existing webhook-only `mapStatus()`), `ReconcilePendingPaymentsCommand` now matches on `$payment->status` (typed) instead of a raw string. `data['status']` still carries the raw value for diagnostics only.

**`ChipGateway`** (`App\Services\Payment\Chip\ChipGateway`) implements all four `PaymentGateway` methods directly from `docs.chip-in.asia/openapi/chip-collect.yaml`, fetched live and cross-checked at each step rather than assumed (this project's own twice-bitten "docs alone aren't enough" discipline, ADR-022 decision 5): Bearer auth, `POST /purchases/` (client/purchase/brand_id/reference/redirect fields, `payment_method_whitelist` for channel selection — confirmed CHIP's FPX is a single generic redirect with no bank-code parameter, customer picks their bank on CHIP's own hosted `checkout_url`, unlike Xendit's 39 per-bank channels — this is the exact fact item 2 of the pointer below needs), `GET /purchases/{id}/` to poll, and `GET /public_key/` (cached, `webhook_public_key_ttl` config) for RSA signature verification. `checkout_url` is normalized into the same `actions: [{type, descriptor: "WEB_URL", value}]` array shape Xendit's real API returns, so `extractCheckoutRedirectUrl()` needs zero CHIP-specific frontend logic. New `config('services.chip')` block + `CHIP_*` env vars; bound as `payment-gateway.chip` in `AppServiceProvider`, alongside the existing `payment-gateway.xendit`. 15 new `ChipGatewayTest` cases (`Http::fake()` only, plus real in-test RSA keypairs for signature verification — valid signature accepted, tampered body rejected, wrong-key signature rejected, missing header rejected, public key fetch cached across calls) + 2 new `PaymentGatewayFactoryTest`/`XenditGatewayTest` cases each. Full backend suite **516/516 green**. Per ADR-022 decision 5: **not yet "verified"** — no live CHIP account exists, `payment_methods.gateway` has no `'chip'` row yet (item 6), and `app:chip-smoke-test` (item 7) hasn't run against a real account. Next: item 5 (`ChipWebhookController`) — the FPX-generic-vs-per-bank fact confirmed above directly resolves item 6's previously-open `method_key` alignment question (task #2's deferred note): CHIP's FPX row should share a `method_key` representing "FPX in general," conflicting with **all** 39 Xendit per-bank FPX rows under the mutual-exclusivity guard, not just one.

**Item 5 shipped, 2026-08-03 (same session) — `ChipWebhookController`, built test-first.** Mirrors `XenditWebhookController`'s order-lifecycle logic (find by `payment_ref`, PAY-2 already-paid guard, queue `FulfillOrderJob` rather than run inline) but resolves `PaymentGatewayFactory::make('chip')` explicitly in its constructor rather than the plain `PaymentGateway::class` default binding, which stays deliberately pinned to Xendit (a webhook route's gateway is fixed by its URL, not resolved per-request — `AppServiceProvider`'s own doc comment). Deliberately **not** refactored into a shared base class with `XenditWebhookController` despite the near-identical post-verification logic — per this project's own "don't prematurely abstract" convention, duplicating ~30 lines of small, well-tested logic costs less than an abstraction that would have to be undone the first time one gateway's webhook handling needs to diverge (e.g. a CHIP-specific event type Xendit has no equivalent of). New route `POST /api/webhooks/chip`, same no-auth treatment as Xendit's (the signature check IS the authentication). 6 new `ChipWebhookControllerTest` cases — genuinely RSA-signed requests driven through the real route and the real `ChipGateway` binding (not a faked gateway), matching `ChipGatewayTest`'s own real-keypair convention, proving the controller, factory resolution, and gateway are actually wired together correctly end-to-end, not just each unit-tested in isolation. Full backend suite **522/522 green**. Next: item 6 (seed CHIP FPX `payment_methods` row, aligning `method_key` with all 39 Xendit FPX rows per the fact confirmed in item 4).

**Item 6 shipped, 2026-08-03 (same session) — CHIP FPX rows seeded, `method_key` design corrected on contact with the actual mechanism.** Re-examining the item-4 plan ("CHIP's FPX row should conflict with all 39 Xendit rows") against the `method_key` guard actually built in item 2 surfaced a real design problem, caught before implementation rather than after: that guard is a strict 1:1 equality check (`updateStatus()` blocks activation only when another row shares the *exact same* `method_key`), which cannot express CHIP's real N:1 relationship (one generic CHIP FPX row vs. 39 distinct Xendit per-bank rows) without either collapsing all 39 Xendit rows onto one shared key — breaking their existing, correct ability to be active simultaneously (multiple named bank buttons, per the founder's own reference screenshot) — or inventing a new "method_group" concept nobody asked for. Corrected decision, confirmed with the founder: CHIP's FPX rows get their **own** distinct `method_key` (`fpx_chip`/`fpx_chip_b2b1`), no automatic conflict against any Xendit row. Switching FPX from Xendit to CHIP is a deliberate admin migration action (manually deactivate the 39 Xendit rows, activate these two) — not a scenario the automatic guard needs to cover, unlike the identical-button risk (e.g. TnG) that guard was actually built for. `PaymentMethodSeeder` gained `seedChip()`: `fpx` (CHIP's own literal channel-code enum, no translation table — same pass-through convention as every Xendit code already stored as-is) at RM1 flat fee, `fpx_b2b1` at RM2 flat fee (chip-in.asia/collect, confirmed 2026-07-30), both `gateway='chip'`, `is_active=false`. Customer-facing labels ("Other Banks (FPX)"/"Other Banks (FPX Business)") deliberately never mention "CHIP" — the gateway name stays backend-only per the public endpoint's own contract (item 3). 1 new `PaymentMethodSeederTest` case; existing all-rows-are-xendit assertion split out since that's no longer true. Seeded against the real local dev DB too (`php artisan db:seed --class=PaymentMethodSeeder`, idempotent — added exactly 2 rows, 46→48, no duplicates). Full backend suite **523/523 green**. Next: item 7 (`app:chip-smoke-test`) — cannot actually be run successfully yet, CHIP merchant account approval still pending (founder-reported, 2026-08-04); the command will be built and ready, but stays unrun/unverified until real `CHIP_SECRET_KEY`/`CHIP_BRAND_ID` exist, per ADR-022 decision 5's own gate.

**Item 7 shipped (code only, unrun), 2026-08-03 (same session) — `app:chip-smoke-test`, closing out ADR-022's full 7-item build plan.** Mirrors `app:xendit-smoke-test`'s shape exactly: creates a real RM1 `fpx` purchase via `ChipGateway`, fetches it back by id, prints both results. One real difference from Xendit's own smoke test, called out in the command's own doc comment rather than silently copied: Xendit's version can sanity-check a `xnd_development_...` key prefix before running; CHIP has **no separate sandbox endpoint and no documented test-key prefix** (ADR-022 decision 5's own unresolved-until-registration item), so this command always hits the real production API and can only warn, not verify, that the configured key is test-mode — confirmed by checking CHIP's docs again, not assumed. No automated test written, matching the existing convention: both `XenditSmokeTest`/`GamevionSmokeTest` are manual/dev-only tools with no test coverage of their own, by design (their entire purpose is exercising the real external API, which `Http::fake()` coverage can't do). Verified registered and fails gracefully with a clear "CHIP_SECRET_KEY / CHIP_BRAND_ID not set" message when run without credentials — confirmed live against the real local backend, not just read as correct. Full backend suite unaffected, **523/523 green**.

**ADR-022's full 7-item CHIP build plan is now code-complete.** What remains is explicitly **not** a code gap: CHIP merchant account approval is pending (founder-reported, 2026-08-04) — until that clears and real `CHIP_SECRET_KEY`/`CHIP_BRAND_ID` exist, `app:chip-smoke-test` cannot actually run, and per ADR-022 decision 5, `ChipGateway` stays unverified and both seeded CHIP `payment_methods` rows stay `is_active=false` — no code in this session bypasses that gate. Once approval lands: set the real env vars, run `php artisan app:chip-smoke-test`, and only after it passes should an admin activate a CHIP row via `/middleware/payment-methods` (itself gated by the `method_key` mutual-exclusivity guard from item 2 for any row sharing a real-world method with another active gateway's row).

**Playwright E2E policy decided, plus a pre-existing "Issue Voucher" UI gap closed, 2026-08-04 (new session), grilled via `/mattpocock-skills:grilling` — closes item 1 of the pointer above, planning-and-one-prerequisite-fix only, `e2e/` scaffold itself not yet built.** Grilling the E2E plan (founder's own opening question: does the suite just keep growing forever, or is there a real policy) surfaced that the third proposed golden path — admin issues a voucher for a failed order (ADR-004's other resolution path) — had a fully-built, fully-tested backend (`VoucherController::storeFromOrder()`, 6 tests, race-safe via a real unique index) but **no admin UI button to trigger it**, deliberately deferred per that code's own comment. Founder's call: build the small missing UI now rather than fake a UI-less test or drop to 2 golden paths. Shipped: `Order::voucher()` relation, `OrderController::show()` eager-loads it, `IssueVoucherModal.tsx` (mirrors `ResendDeliveryModal`'s pattern) + the button on a failed order (hidden once `order.voucher` is set), `lib/orders.ts`'s `issueVoucherFromOrder()`. 2 new backend tests; full suite **525/525 green**; `tsc --noEmit`/`next build` clean on `admin/`.

The E2E policy itself is now recorded in full as **[ADR-023](./adr.md)** — not an addendum to ADR-020, since it governs ongoing suite behavior rather than correcting that ADR's original scope. Summary: 3 golden-path tests (checkout→payment→order status; admin login→Resend Delivery; admin login→Issue Voucher), Chromium only; growth bounded to two named triggers (a genuinely new payment-gateway/resolution-mechanism, or a real production incident traced to a pure UI regression PHPUnit couldn't see) enforced via a PR-template checklist, not a CI gate; zero-tolerance flakiness (fix-or-delete same PR, no quarantine lane); Gamevion faked forever at the HTTP layer (its sandbox is permanently broken per ADR-006), Xendit hit for real against its working sandbox with the webhook simulated via a directly-signed POST; a dedicated minimal E2E boot script (not `scripts/dev.sh`, to avoid inheriting its known dev-tooling gotchas); a thin `e2e/fixtures` file built on existing Eloquent patterns, owned by whichever PR changes the relevant schema. Concrete next action: scaffold `e2e/` itself (Playwright config, the 3 spec files, the fixture file, wiring a `playwright` job into the already-existing `.github/workflows/ci.yml`) — not done yet as of this entry.

**`e2e/` scaffolded and all 3 golden-path tests verified passing for real, same session, 2026-08-04 — closes the concrete next action above.** `e2e/playwright.config.ts` (Chromium only, 3 `webServer` entries — backend via `e2e/scripts/boot-backend.sh`, admin/storefront via `next dev`, all on `localhost` not `127.0.0.1` — see below), `backend/database/seeders/E2ESeeder.php` (reuses `DatabaseSeeder` as-is, adds one Game+Package with no player-validator, one active `AMBANK_FPX` channel, two separate pre-existing failed orders so the Resend/Voucher tests never contend), and the 3 spec files under `e2e/tests/`. Ran `npx playwright test` for real, not just written-and-assumed-correct — found and fixed 4 real bugs along the way:

1. **`php artisan serve` silently ignores exported env vars unless `--no-reload` is passed** (`ServeCommand::$passthroughVariables` — a small hardcoded whitelist) — `boot-backend.sh`'s own `DB_DATABASE` override was being dropped, so the "e2e" backend was actually serving requests against the real local dev database. Caught because the admin Orders search returned real production order data instead of the seeded fixtures. Fixed with `--no-reload`; now a third `AGENTS.md` gotcha, since this bites any script booting `serve` against env vars set outside `.env`, not just this one.
2. **Next.js 16 dev-mode cross-origin blocking** — Playwright's default `127.0.0.1` didn't match `config/cors.php`'s `ADMIN_URL`/`STOREFRONT_URL` defaults (`http://localhost:3000/3001`), silently blocking every browser-originated API call including login. Fixed by standardizing `e2e/`'s own URLs on `localhost` to match the existing CORS config, not by adding `allowedDevOrigins` (tried first, then reverted once the real mismatch was found — the origin needed to match what the backend already trusted, not be more permissively whitelisted).
3. **Reading a response body after the page navigates away from it fails** — `OrderForm.tsx`'s `handleConfirmPayment()` calls `window.location.assign(redirectUrl)` immediately once `submitCheckout()` resolves, which reliably beat Playwright's own `page.waitForResponse(...).json()` in a race to read the `/api/checkout` response body. Fixed via `page.route()` + `route.fetch()`, which reads the body independently of what the page does with the response next.
4. **Locator ambiguity from Playwright's default substring name-matching** — `getByRole('button', {name: 'Continue'})` also matched a disabled sidebar button whose full text was "Complete steps to continue" (contains "continue" as a substring); `getByRole('button', {name: 'Issue Voucher'})` also matched the "Issue Voucher…" trigger button once the modal was open (background stays mounted under the overlay). Both fixed with `exact: true`.

All 3 tests pass together (~20s total) against a real, throwaway, freshly-migrated-and-seeded backend — including the storefront checkout test reaching a real `201` from `POST /api/checkout`, a real `bank-web-mock.xendit.co` redirect (this local dev environment's `backend/.env` already has a working Xendit test-mode key), a simulated webhook, and the order status page showing "Delivered." `.github/workflows/ci.yml` gained a `playwright` job (deliberately not in `build-and-push`'s `needs:`, matching ADR-020 decision #9's "not required to gate deploy") — it will pass the 2 admin specs today but fail the checkout spec until `XENDIT_TEST_SECRET_KEY` is added as a repo secret, the same kind of "structurally complete, blocked on an unprovisioned secret" gap `build-and-push` itself already carries for `DIGITALOCEAN_ACCESS_TOKEN`. Also shipped this same session, as ADR-023's own grilling surfaced it as a prerequisite: the "Issue Voucher" admin UI button (`Order::voucher()` relation, `IssueVoucherModal.tsx`, `lib/orders.ts`'s `issueVoucherFromOrder()`) — see the entry above. Full detail in [ADR-023's newest addendum](./adr.md).

**Voucher-at-checkout shipped (ADR-024), 2026-08-13 (new session) — grilled live with the founder (`/mattpocock-skills:grilling`), then built end-to-end same session, no scope changes on contact with code.** Prompted by the founder reviewing the existing Issue Voucher flow live and finding two real gaps: no admin voucher-detail page (`/admin/vouchers` was list+create only), and — the bigger one — `VoucherService::redeem()` had existed since the original Voucher build but was never wired to checkout at all, confirmed by `CheckoutController`'s own doc comment saying so. Grilling covered redemption timing (locks only *after* the gateway confirms a real payment request — never before, since ADR-004's no-cash-refund policy makes a post-payment voucher failure unrecoverable), the wallet usage model (partial spend across many orders, new `voucher_redemptions` audit table), server-side ownership lock (email/phone match, ORD-9), the full-cover edge case (skip the gateway entirely, force `transaction_fee=0` — closes a real gap where a flat-fee channel would otherwise still charge its fee against a $0 cash portion), and exactly two restore triggers (payment never completes, or delivery is abandoned via the admin's own "Issue Voucher" give-up action — never on a merely-Failed-but-still-resendable delivery). Full grilled design and shipped-addendum in **[ADR-024](./adr.md)**, not duplicated here.

Backend: `voucher_redemptions` table, `vouchers.customer_phone`/`orders.voucher_id` columns, `VoucherService` rewritten (`preview()`/`redeem()`/`commit()`/`restore()`), `CheckoutService`/`CheckoutController` wired (new public `POST /api/vouchers/preview` backs the storefront's Apply button), restore wired at both webhook controllers' terminal-Failed branch and `ReconcilePendingPaymentsCommand`'s own, `VoucherController::storeFromOrder()` now restores-and-issues atomically, and a found-during-build guard (`Admin\OrderController::retryDelivery()`/`resend()` now reject an order that already has a voucher — closes a pre-existing double-compensation gap unrelated to this feature's own new code). Admin: `/admin/vouchers` gained a real detail view (usage history, stat cards) via the same selected-state pattern `/admin/orders` already uses — this codebase still has no `[id]/page.tsx` route anywhere. Storefront: `ReviewModal.tsx`'s new Apply field + `OrderForm.tsx` wiring, the existing "no redirect URL → push to order status" fallback already handled a full-cover order's empty `payment_actions` for free.

25 new/changed backend tests, full fast suite **548/548 green**; a rewritten `VoucherRedeemConcurrencyTest` (4/4 green against real MySQL) proves the exact "two browser tabs, same voucher" race the founder asked about is safe. Migrations verified via `SHOW INDEX FROM` on real MySQL and applied to the local dev DB (the documented gotcha, handled not hit blind). `npx tsc --noEmit`/`eslint`/`next build` clean on both `admin/` and `storefront/`.

**Live-testing found and fixed one real gap, then surfaced an unrelated third-party fix — same session, 2026-08-13/14.** Founder live-tested the shipped voucher-at-checkout feature for real: the storefront's Apply/discount preview worked, but the real `POST /api/checkout` that followed showed no discount at all on the resulting order (`voucher_id`/`voucher_discount` both empty). Diagnosed by direct API reproduction (bypassing the storefront entirely) rather than guessing: a raw `curl` with an explicit `voucher_code` produced the exact correct discount, fee, and — once a real gateway call was let through end-to-end — a real `voucher_redemptions` row and a correctly decremented voucher balance. **Confirms the backend built for ADR-024 is fully correct**; the reported symptom was a stale storefront dev-server bundle, not a code defect — resolved by the founder restarting `npm run dev`, no code change needed.

While diagnosing that, the founder's own real order reached `delivered` through **Gamevion sandbox** (`GAMEVION_SANDBOX=true`) — which shouldn't have been possible per `ADR-006`'s long-standing "sandbox permanently broken" finding. Re-verified deliberately rather than assumed a fluke: real production `product_code`s (Free Fire + MLBB, 3 different SKUs) now complete successfully in sandbox; genuinely bogus codes still correctly `422` (real validation, not a blanket accept); Gamevion's own advertised sandbox-simulation code (`234`) still `422`s, a separate minor quirk. **Gamevion appears to have fixed their sandbox `/api/order` bug on their own side** — full evidence trail in [ADR-006's newest addendum](./adr.md). `/api/product`'s sandbox catalog is unchanged (still one hardcoded item), so the Price Sync/Deactivation-Detection gotcha (2026-07-27 addendum) is **not** resolved — still don't run a real Price Sync while `GAMEVION_SANDBOX=true`. §15's Supplier Adapter row updated to match.

**Security review of checkout/payment-confirmation paths — 2026-08-14 (same session).** Founder asked for a targeted security audit: can a third party manipulate the storefront price, or forge a "paid" signal to the payment gateway. Full code read (not diff-based — no committed baseline to diff against for this scope) confirmed the core money paths already hold up: `CheckoutController`/`CheckoutService`/`PricingService`/`CheckoutTotalService` never trust a client-submitted price or discount (`Package.cost_price`/`reseller_cost_price` resolved server-side, voucher discount resolved server-side from the code alone, ORD-9 holding in practice not just on paper); `XenditGateway`/`ChipGateway` both verify webhook authenticity (`hash_equals` token compare / RSA-SHA256 against CHIP's own fetched public key — neither forgeable without the real secret); `TrackOrderController` exposes no internal financial fields. One real gap found: `PaymentWebhookEvent::$amountSen` (parsed by both webhook controllers) and `PaymentResponse::$data['amount_sen']` (returned by `ReconcilePendingPaymentsCommand`'s own `getPayment()` pull) were parsed but never compared against `Order::$final_amount` before marking an order Paid and dispatching fulfillment — defense-in-depth gap, not a proven live exploit (`payment_ref` already binds one webhook to one fixed-amount request created server-side, and a gateway's own signature-verified SUCCEEDED/paid status already implies the full amount was received). Fixed in `XenditWebhookController`, `ChipWebhookController`, and `ReconcilePendingPaymentsCommand::recover()` — a mismatch now rejects (webhooks: `409`, order stays Pending) or skips (reconcile: logs `error`, order stays Pending) instead of trusting status alone. 3 new tests (1 per site), full fast suite 551/551 green. No ADR opened — this closes a gap in an already-decided design (PAY-1/PAY-2's existing "most security-sensitive boundary" framing, ORD-9), not a new architectural trade-off.
